<a id="pm-s1"></a>
## 1. Abstract and result contract

> Under the finite-level Ramanujan–Arthur decomposition stated in Theorem 1.1, we construct a global Arthur enhancement for every occurring cuspidal excursion parameter at a specified full finite level for a split connected semisimple group over a global function field. A single algebraic SL<sub>2</sub> and a commuting Weil centralizer map recover the given parameter by diagonal specialization on the entire Weil group, including inertia. The result does not assert ellipticity, Arthur-packet classification, or a multiplicity formula.

- **claim-local-arthur-orbit** (classification, [source](#stmt-thm-parent)): For every occurring excursion character $\nu$, there is a unique nilpotent $L$-orbit $\mathcal O_\nu\subset\operatorname{Lie}L$ such that, at every closed point $x\in U$, the semisimple class of $\sigma_\nu(\operatorname{Frob}_x)$ has the form $$\label{intro:local-arthur} \left[ \phi_{\mathcal O_\nu} \begin{pmatrix}a^{d_x}&0\\0&a^{-d_x}\end{pmatrix}c_x \right].$$ Here $\phi_{\mathcal O_\nu}:\operatorname{SL}_2\to L$ is a Jacobson–Morozov homomorphism for $\mathcal O_\nu$ and $c_x$ is semisimple, centralizes its image, and is algebraic and conjugate into a compact subgroup at every complex embedding. The same orbit occurs at every $x$.
  - Explanation: The spectral input assigns a unique nilpotent orbit to each occurring excursion character and uses that same orbit in the semisimple Frobenius decomposition at every good place. The commuting compact factors are local choices.
- **claim-global-arthur-enhancement** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-thm-main)): Assume Theorem <a href="#thm:parent" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:parent">1</a>. For every $X,G,D,\ell,j$ and every occurring excursion character $\nu$ as above, there are an algebraic homomorphism $$\phi_\nu:\operatorname{SL}_{2,k}\longrightarrow L$$ and a continuous homomorphism $$\tau_\nu:\Gamma\longrightarrow Z_L\bigl(\phi_\nu(\operatorname{SL}_2)\bigr)(k),$$ both defined over a finite extension of $\mathbb Q_\ell$, with the following properties.* 1. *The differential of $\phi_\nu$ sends $\begin{psmallmatrix}0&1\\0&0\end{psmallmatrix}$ to $\mathcal O_\nu$.* 2. *The Zariski closure of $\tau_\nu(\Gamma)$ is reductive. For every finite-dimensional algebraic representation $r$ of the split rational form of $L$ and every closed point $x\in U$, each eigenvalue of $r(\tau_\nu(\operatorname{Frob}_x))$ lies in $j(\overline\mathbb Q)$ and its inverse image under $j$ has absolute value one under every embedding $\overline\mathbb Q\hookrightarrow\mathbb C$.* 3. *After one fixed $L(k)$-conjugation of $\sigma_\nu$, $$\label{intro:global-specialization} \sigma_\nu(w)=\tau_\nu(w)\, \phi_\nu\begin{pmatrix}a^{\deg(w)}&0\\0&a^{-\deg(w)}\end{pmatrix} \qquad(w\in\Gamma).$$* *The centralizer is the full algebraic centralizer; it may be disconnected.
  - Explanation: Assuming the finite-level decomposition, an algebraic Arthur factor and a commuting continuous Weil homomorphism recover the specified excursion parameter after a fixed conjugation. The Weil factor has reductive closure and algebraic Frobenius eigenvalues of absolute value one at every complex embedding. Specialization holds on the entire Weil group.
- **claim-shtuka-cohomological-inputs** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-glob-cohomology)): The groups $H^b_{J,V}$ have the following properties.* 1. *Their cohomology sheaves are ind-smooth on $(U^\circ)^J$. Geometric transport and partial Frobenius give an action of $\Gamma^J$ on the transported fibers, compatible with fusion. When several legs are fused, the Weil group acts diagonally on those legs.* 2. *For every auxiliary closed point $y\in \|U^\circ\|$, the generic fiber of $H^b_{J,V}$ is finitely generated over the spherical Hecke algebra $\mathcal H_y$. This action commutes with the partial Weil actions. Quotients by finite-codimension ideals of $\mathcal H_y$ are finite-dimensional continuous Weil representations, defined over finite extensions of $\mathbb Q_\ell$ whenever the data are so defined.* 3. *The identity expressing a spherical Hecke operator as creation, partial Frobenius, and annihilation holds with arbitrary spectator legs, on the full cohomology. On cusp vectors its excursion operations agree with those defining $\mathcal B_D$.
  - Explanation: Shtuka cohomology carries ind-smooth transport, compatible partial Weil actions, Hecke finite generation, and the creation–Frobenius–annihilation identity with spectator legs. Finite Hecke quotients provide continuous finite-dimensional Weil representations.
- **claim-complete-frobenius-weight-bound** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-glob-weights)): Let the legs be at pairwise distinct points of $U^\circ$ rational over $\mathbb F_{q^d}$, with external Satake labels. Every eigenvalue of complete $q^d$-Frobenius on a finite-dimensional subquotient of $H^b_{J,V}$ has weight at most $b$.
  - Explanation: At distinct rational leg positions, complete Frobenius weights on finite-dimensional subquotients are bounded above by the compact-cohomology degree.
- **claim-universal-matrix-action** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-glob-matrices)): The matrices $T_V(w)$ define an $A$-equivariant action of $\mathscr B_\Gamma$ on each $M_b^{\mathrm{reg}}$, with $A$ acting on $\operatorname{Hom}(\Gamma,A)$ by conjugation. For a good closed point $y$, the function $\operatorname{Tr}_V(\mathbf b(\operatorname{Frob}_y))$ acts by the spherical Hecke operator with label $V$.
  - Explanation: Partial Weil matrices satisfy the tensor and group-law relations defining the universal representation algebra. Invariant Frobenius traces act as the corresponding spherical Hecke operators.
- **claim-commuting-degree-two-coordinate** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-glob-degree-two)): The morphism <a href="#eq:degree-two" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:degree-two">[eq:degree-two]</a> induces an equivariant map $$E_b:M_b^{\mathrm{reg}}\longrightarrow M_{b+2}^{\mathrm{reg}}\otimes\mathfrak g.$$ Its coordinates commute with each other and with the $\mathscr B_\Gamma$-action. Writing $\mathbf e$ for the resulting Lie-algebra coordinate, so that its linear functions have degree two, one has the identity of operators $$\label{eq:global-relation} \operatorname{Ad}(\mathbf b(w))\mathbf e =q^{\deg(w)}\mathbf e \qquad(w\in\Gamma).$$ These operations commute with all good-place Hecke actions.
  - Explanation: Derived Satake supplies a commuting Lie-algebra coordinate whose adjoint Weil transformation is prescribed by degree. Its construction remains compatible with good-place Hecke operations.
- **claim-framed-local-coordinates** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-glob-frames)): As graded rational $A^n$-modules, $$\label{eq:framed-module} \mathcal M_n\simeq\operatorname{Ind}_A^{A^n}M_\bullet^{\mathrm{reg}} =\bigl(\mathcal O(A^n)\otimes M_\bullet^{\mathrm{reg}}\bigr)^A.$$ If $g_i\in A$ are the frame coordinates, with diagonal change of frame $g_i\mapsto g_i h^{-1}$, then the adjoint holonomy and degree-two coordinates act as $$\label{eq:framed-action} s_{i,\mathrm{ad}}=g_i\mathbf b(\gamma_i)g_i^{-1}, \qquad e_i=g_i\mathbf e g_i^{-1}.$$
  - Explanation: The multi-place spectral module is induced from the regular module. Independent frames conjugate the universal holonomy and Lie coordinate into the local coordinates, retaining full matrices rather than only traces.
- **claim-finite-type-cyclic-support** (construction, [source](#stmt-prop-global-support)): The algebra $R$ is a nonzero finitely generated graded $k$-algebra with rational $A$-action. It has a universal homomorphism $\mathbf b:\Gamma\to A(R)$ and an equivariant element $\mathbf e\in\mathfrak g\otimes R$ satisfying <a href="#eq:global-relation" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:global-relation">[eq:global-relation]</a>. There is an equivariant graded injection $$\label{eq:cyclic-support} R\hookrightarrow M_\bullet^{\mathrm{reg}}, \qquad 1\longmapsto f.$$ For every finite tuple $(w_i)_{i\in I}$ and every $F\in\mathcal O(A^I)^A$, its value on the universal tuple is the scalar $$F\bigl((\mathbf b(w_i))_{i\in I}\bigr) =F\bigl((\sigma_{\nu,\mathrm{ad}}(w_i))_{i\in I}\bigr) \quad\text{in }R.$$ In particular the full simultaneous-conjugacy data of the excursion parameter are retained by $\operatorname{Spec}R$.
  - Explanation: The cyclic algebra generated by the cuspidal eigenvector is nonzero and of finite type. Its universal homomorphism and Lie coordinate obey the scaling equation, and all simultaneous tuple invariants match the specified adjoint excursion parameter.
- **claim-connected-centralizer-test-places** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-test-places)): There are infinitely many distinct points $x_i\in \|U_0\|\setminus\{y_0\}$, with $d_i=\deg(x_i)$ divisible by $\|\Omega\|$, such that, for $t=t_{x_i}$,* 1. *$H=Z_L(t)$ and $Z_A(t_{\mathrm{ad}})$ are connected;* 2. *near the class of $t_{\mathrm{ad}}$, the map $L/\!/L\to A/\!/A$ is a $Z(L)$-torsor, and the central torsor $L\to A$ is its pullback. In particular, $Z_A(t_{\mathrm{ad}})=H/Z(L)$.
  - Explanation: Infinitely many good places satisfy the required degree divisibility, connectedness of both centralizers, and the central-torsor property near the selected adjoint conjugacy class.
- **claim-open-resonance-orbit** (classification, [source](#stmt-lem-orbit-open)): The cocharacter $m$ is central in $H$, and $E_t$ has $m$-weight two. Every element of $E_t$ is nilpotent and admits a triple compatible with $t$. There are finitely many $H$-orbits in $E_t$. The raising element supplied by Theorem <a href="#thm:parent" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:parent">1</a> lies in the unique open $H$-orbit, and its stabilizer in $H$ is reductive. These assertions include the zero orbit, when $E_t=0$.
  - Explanation: The resonance space consists of compatible nilpotent elements and has finitely many centralizer orbits. The prescribed raising element occupies the unique open orbit with reductive stabilizer; the statement includes the degenerate zero resonance space.
- **claim-simultaneous-tuple-conjugacy** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-lem-orbit-tuples)): Let $B$ be a connected reductive group over an algebraically closed field of characteristic zero, and let $\Delta$ be an abstract group. Suppose $\rho_1,\rho_2:\Delta\to B(k)$ have reductive Zariski closures. If every finite tuple has the same values under all simultaneous conjugation-invariant regular functions for $\rho_1$ and $\rho_2$, then the two homomorphisms are conjugate by one element of $B(k)$.
  - Explanation: For homomorphisms with reductive closures, equality of all simultaneous invariant evaluations on finite tuples produces a single conjugator for the entire abstract group.
- **claim-open-orbit-global-reconstruction** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-prop-open-orbit-enhancement)): Let $x$ be a place selected in Lemma <a href="#lem:test-places" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:test-places">9</a> and $z\in\operatorname{Spec}R(k)$. Write $b:\Gamma\to A(k)$ and $e\in\mathfrak g$ for the specialization of $\mathbf b$ and $\mathbf e$ at $z$. Conjugate so that the semisimple part of $b(\operatorname{Frob}_x)$ is $t_{x,\mathrm{ad}}$. If $e\in E_{t_x}^{\mathrm{op}}$, then there exist $\phi_\nu$ and $\tau_\nu$ with all the properties of Theorem <a href="#thm:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:main">2</a>: the prescribed orbit, reductive closure, finite field of definition, continuity on $W_U$, purity at every complex embedding, and equality with the given parameter on the entire Weil group.
  - Explanation: A support point reaching the open resonance orbit at a selected place suffices to recover the full global enhancement. Levi projection preserves tuple invariants, simultaneous conjugacy recovers the given parameter, and lifting through the finite center supplies continuity and purity.
- **claim-boundary-under-nonenhancement** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-cor-orbit-boundary)): If the enhancement in Theorem <a href="#thm:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:main">2</a> does not exist for $\nu$, then at every selected place $x_i$ and every geometric point of $\operatorname{Spec}R$, the coordinate $\mathbf e$ belongs to $\partial E_{t_{x_i}}$ after alignment of the semisimple holonomy with $t_{x_i,\mathrm{ad}}$.
  - Explanation: Nonexistence of the enhancement forces every geometric support point into the resonance boundary at every selected place after semisimple alignment.
- **claim-enhanced-iwahori-equivalence** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-enhanced-iwahori)): There is an enhanced monoidal equivalence $\Theta:\mathscr H\simeq\mathscr C$, together with a monoidal intertwining of $\operatorname{Fr}^{d*}$ and $r_*$, having the following properties. There are identifications $$\Theta Z(V)\simeq D(V)\qquad(V\in\operatorname{Rep}(L)),$$ natural for ordinary representation morphisms, and an element $z\in Z(L)(k)$ such that the transported Weil isomorphism on $D(V)$ is its preferred isomorphism multiplied by $V(z)$. Under these identifications, the tensor maps between the $Z(V)$ become constant $L$-equivariant representation maps. On $\operatorname{Rep}(A)\subset\operatorname{Rep}(L)$ they can be chosen to be the ordinary tensor maps. Finally, the central interchange of $Z(V)$ with $Z(W)$ becomes the standard interchange whenever at least one of $V,W$ is neutral.
  - Explanation: The Iwahori comparison becomes an enhanced associative monoidal equivalence compatible with Frobenius and dilation. Central representation kernels have a common central Weil correction, ordinary neutral tensor maps, and standard interchange when a label is neutral.
- **claim-frobenius-dilation-normalization** (theorem, [source](#stmt-enh-frobenius-normalization)): The equivalence <a href="#eq:bezrukavnikov" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:bezrukavnikov">[eq:bezrukavnikov]</a> admits a natural isomorphism of exact functors $$\Phi\operatorname{Fr}^*\simeq (\mu_q)_*\Phi, \qquad \mu_q(N)=qN.$$ Here $\operatorname{Fr}^*$ has the split Weil normalization in which geometric Frobenius acts on $k(-1)$ by $q$.
  - Explanation: Geometric Frobenius corresponds to pushforward under literal nilpotent dilation, with the split Tate normalization retained on shifted morphisms.
- **claim-central-object-identification** (theorem, [source](#stmt-enh-central-objects)): At the level of objects, $\Phi Z(V)\simeq D(V)$ for every $V\in\operatorname{Rep}(L)$.
  - Explanation: The triangulated comparison identifies each central Satake object with its diagonal coherent representation kernel. This is an object-level identification.
- **claim-pure-torsion-free-word-homs** (theorem, [source](#stmt-enh-pure-homs)): For two IC words $P,Q$, the group $\operatorname{Hom}^j_{\mathscr H}(P,Q)$ is pure of weight $j$. Their total graded Hom is a finitely generated, torsion-free $S_T$-module. The same assertions hold when either word is a concatenation of several prescribed words.
  - Explanation: Morphisms between IC convolution words are pure in their cohomological degree. Their graded Hom modules are finitely generated and torsion free over torus equivariant cohomology, including concatenated words.
- **claim-localized-standard-decomposition** (classification, [source](#stmt-enh-localized-standards)): The localization of graded Hom spaces from $S_T$ to $K_T$ is compatible with convolution. In the localized category, $\Delta_w\simeq\nabla_w$; distinct standard objects are orthogonal; and every object is a finite direct sum of shifts of standard objects. Within each connected component, any two standards are linked, before localization, by a chain of nonzero homogeneous morphisms between standards.
  - Explanation: Equivariant localization respects convolution, identifies standards with costandards, and makes distinct standards orthogonal. Objects split into finite sums of shifted standards, while unlocalized homogeneous morphisms connect standards within a component.
- **claim-monoidal-frobenius-intertwiner** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-enh-monoidal-frobenius)): If $\|\Omega\|\mid d$, the exact-functor intertwiner between $\Phi\operatorname{Fr}^{d*}$ and $r_*\Phi$ can be chosen to respect convolution and the unit on the complete graded Hom diagram of IC words.
  - Explanation: The selected degree divisibility removes the scalar tensor defect of the Frobenius intertwiner, making it compatible with convolution and the unit throughout the graded IC-word diagram.
- **claim-pure-diagram-formality** (theorem, [source](#stmt-enh-pure-formality)): In the derived category of complexes over $k$ with one automorphism, consider the full subcategory consisting of complexes with bounded below, degreewise finite-dimensional cohomology, pure of weight $j$ in degree $j$. Its mapping spaces are discrete, and cohomology is an equivalence from this subcategory to the category of graded pure modules. This equivalence is symmetric monoidal for tensor over $k$ with the diagonal automorphism. Consequently, a diagram of mapping complexes of this kind, including all its multilinear composition and tensor operations, is determined by its cohomology diagram.
  - Explanation: For bounded-below complexes with degreewise finite pure cohomology of matching weight and degree, mapping spaces are discrete and cohomology determines the full tensor diagram. Equal-weight extensions need not vanish in the ambient derived category.
- **claim-enhanced-monoidal-lift** (construction, [source](#stmt-enh-lift)): The equivalence on the graded diagram of IC words, with the Frobenius intertwiner from Lemma <a href="#enh:monoidal-frobenius" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="enh:monoidal-frobenius">19</a>, lifts to an enhanced monoidal equivalence $\Theta:\mathscr H\simeq\mathscr C$ intertwining $\operatorname{Fr}^{d*}$ and $r_*$. It induces the prescribed graded Hom identifications on the words.
  - Explanation: Purity identifies the enriched IC-word diagrams, including composition, tensor operations, and Frobenius. Passing to stable idempotent-complete envelopes extends this comparison to the enhanced monoidal categories.
- **claim-mixed-central-weight-comparison** (theorem, [source](#stmt-enh-mixed-central-comparison)): For every $V\in\operatorname{Rep}L$, the enhanced equivalence constructed in Proposition <a href="#enh:lift" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="enh:lift">21</a> satisfies $$\Theta(Z(V))\simeq\Phi(Z(V))\simeq D(V)$$ as geometric objects. The first isomorphism can be chosen to respect the transported Weil structures and the identifications of pure weight pieces supplied by the comparison on IC generators.
  - Explanation: The enhanced and triangulated comparisons agree on mixed central objects with compatible Weil structures and pure pieces. The proof extends the generator comparison through the weight filtration.
- **claim-central-weil-tensor-normalization** (construction, [source](#stmt-enh-central-normalization)): There are identifications $\Theta(Z(V))\simeq D(V)$, natural and additive in $V\in\operatorname{Rep}L$, and an element $z\in Z(L)$ with the following properties. Representation morphisms become their ordinary maps on diagonal representation kernels, and the transported Weil map on $D(V)$ is $$\label{enh:central-weil} \rho_V(z)\,\eta_V:r_*D(V)\longrightarrow D(V).$$ The transported tensor maps are constant; on neutral representations they can be taken to be the ordinary diagonal convolution identifications. The half-braid of $Z(V)$ with $Z(W)$ becomes the ordinary interchange whenever either $V$ or $W$ is neutral.
  - Explanation: Natural additive central-object identifications make representation maps ordinary and Weil maps a preferred identification multiplied by a central element. Tensor maps are constant, become ordinary on neutral labels, and the specified interchanges are standard.
- **claim-horizontal-trace-coend** (theorem, [source](#stmt-trace-coend)): For a rigid enhanced kernel category with monoidal automorphism $\Phi$, the images of kernels compactly generate its presentable trace and generate its horizontal trace under finite colimits and retracts. Moreover, $$\label{eq:horizontal-trace} \operatorname{Map}([J],[J']) \simeq \int^{T}\operatorname{Map}(\Phi T*J,J'*T).$$ The formula applies to the matrix category with levels $I,K$. Each corner trace embeds fully faithfully in the matrix trace. At a closed point of degree $d$, the cyclic trace of its $d$ geometric factors is identified with the one-factor trace twisted by $\operatorname{Fr}^{d*}$. This identification respects level transfers and full-cycle slides.
  - Explanation: Derived coends compute trace mapping complexes. Kernel images generate the trace, corner traces embed fully faithfully, and a closed-point cycle contracts to a trace twisted by the corresponding Frobenius power while retaining transfers and slides.
- **claim-affine-hecke-trace-algebra** (theorem, [source](#stmt-trace-hecke-algebra)): The cohomology of $\operatorname{End}([\mathbf1_I])$ is concentrated in degree zero and is the affine Hecke algebra with parameter $r$. Its unshifted standard Weil classes are the characteristic-function generators in the normalization of [[Parent](#ref-Parent), Proposition 2.6, ``Hecke action and transfer''].
  - Explanation: The trace-unit endomorphism cohomology is the ordinary affine Hecke algebra. Unshifted standard Weil classes match characteristic-function generators with the prescribed normalization.
- **claim-coherent-trace-spherical-transfer** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-trace-model)): The equivalence of Theorem <a href="#thm:enhanced-iwahori" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:enhanced-iwahori">14</a> identifies the Iwahori horizontal trace with $\operatorname{Coh}^L(Y_N)$, carrying $[\mathbf1_I]$ to $P_N$ and $[Z(V)]$ to $P_N\otimes V$. Sheaf duality $\mathbb D=\operatorname{RHom}(-,\mathcal O)$ is a duality on this coherent model, and $\mathbb DP_N\simeq P_N$.* *Let $z\in Z(L)$ be the element of Theorem <a href="#thm:enhanced-iwahori" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:enhanced-iwahori">14</a>. Full-cycle slide on the representation factor is $V(zs)$. Hyperspecial transfer identifies $[S(V)]$ with $P_N^S\otimes V$, where $P_N^S$ is a retract of $P_N$. If $s_{\rm sph}$ denotes spherical holonomy, then $$\label{eq:coordinate-shift} s_{\rm sph}=zs.$$ For neutral labels this comparison respects representation morphisms, homogeneous spherical-kernel morphisms, and matrix operations obtained from slides by composition, contraction, and tensoring by neutral spectators. It also respects invariant holonomy functions coming from arbitrary representations of $L$.
  - Explanation: The Iwahori trace is realized by equivariant coherent complexes on the derived equation space. Its unit is the self-dual Springer object; central labels become representation bundles. Spherical transfer identifies a retract and compares holonomy matrices, homogeneous maps, and neutral spectators with the central coordinate correction.
- **claim-strongly-etale-semisimple-slice** (construction, [source](#stmt-local-semisimple-slice)): There is an affine saturated $H$-invariant open neighborhood $U_H\subset H$ of the identity class such that the conjugation map $$\label{local:eq-slice-map} L\mathbin{\times}^{H}(tU_H)\longrightarrow L, \qquad [g,tu]\longmapsto gtu g^{-1},$$ is strongly étale over its image. More precisely, it is the pullback of the induced étale map $U_H/\!/H\to L/\!/L$ along the adjoint quotient. The analogous statement holds with $t$ replaced by $z^{-1}t$. The neighborhood can be chosen so that the transverse conjugation operator and all nonresonant blocks of the vector equations are invertible. It contains the entire unipotent locus of $H$. The identity point of $U_H/\!/H$ is the only point above the selected semisimple class; the corresponding fiber in $U_H$ still contains every unipotent element.
  - Explanation: A saturated affine centralizer slice is strongly étale over its image and retains the entire unipotent locus. Its shrinking makes transverse and nonresonant equation blocks invertible without replacing unipotent group elements by the identity.
- **claim-classical-complete-intersection-models** (theorem, [source](#stmt-local-equation-models)): The algebra $R_-$ and the shear of $B_{\mathrm{sph}}^0$ have cohomology only in degree zero. Their classical schemes are complete intersections of dimension $\dim H$.
  - Explanation: The negative equation algebra and sheared spherical algebra are classical complete intersections. Finitely many resonance orbits give the expected equation codimension.
- **claim-covariant-graded-koszul-embedding** (construction, [source](#stmt-local-graded-koszul)): For the algebra $R_-$ in <a href="#local:eq-negative-algebra" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="local:eq-negative-algebra">[local:eq-negative-algebra]</a>, rational internal derived Hom from the augmentation $A_0$ followed by shear defines an exact fully faithful functor $$\label{local:eq-koszul-functor} \mathcal K:\operatorname{Coh}^H(R_-)\longrightarrow\operatorname{Coh}^H(Y).$$ Before shear the functor is $A_0$-linear and commutes with tensoring by finite-dimensional $H$-representations. It extends fully faithfully to the Ind-categories of these coherent categories.
  - Explanation: Rational internal derived Hom from the augmentation, followed by shear, embeds coherent negative-model objects fully faithfully in the positive model. The unsheared functor retains base scalars and finite representation tensors, and the coherent embedding extends to Ind-categories.
- **claim-derived-flag-slice** (classification, [source](#stmt-local-flag-slice)): Over $U_H$, the flag space defining $P_N$ is the disjoint union, over $b$, of the derived schemes $$\label{local:eq-negative-flag} \{(B,u,N):(B,u)\in U_b,\ N\in W_B, \ (\operatorname{Ad}(u)-1)N=0\text{ in }W_B\}.$$ The base $U_b$ is smooth of dimension $\dim H$. All derived structure in <a href="#local:eq-negative-flag" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="local:eq-negative-flag">[local:eq-negative-flag]</a> is in its displayed vector equation.
  - Explanation: The sliced Springer flag space decomposes by flag type. Each base is smooth, and all derived structure remains in the explicit vector equation rather than in flag incidence.
- **claim-koszul-image-of-springer-flags** (theorem, [source](#stmt-local-flag-image)): The functor $\mathcal K$ sends $P_N$ to $P$, without a shift or an equivariant line twist. It sends $\mathbb D P_N$ to the same object, using the self-duality of $P_N$ in the coherent trace model.
  - Explanation: The Koszul transform of the Springer object is the sum of positive derived flag direct images. The calculation cancels determinant characters and shifts and also applies to the self-dual Springer object.
- **claim-polynomial-operation-representatives** (theorem, [source](#stmt-local-polynomial-representatives)): Put $C=\mathcal O(L)^L$ and $C'=\mathcal O(U_H)^H$, with the map $C\to C'$ given by the spherical coordinate $s_{\rm sph}=tu$. Write $B_{\rm sph}=\operatorname{sh}H^\bullet(B_{\rm sph}^0)$ for the classical spherical equation algebra, retaining polynomial degree as the original cohomological degree divided by two. For neutral representations $V,V'$ the restriction and quotient map $$\label{local:eq-polynomial-surjection} \bigl(C'\otimes_C (\mathcal O(L)\otimes\operatorname{Sym}(\mathfrak g^*)\otimes V^*\otimes V')\bigr)^L \longrightarrow (B_{\rm sph}\otimes V^*\otimes V')^H$$ is surjective and preserves polynomial degree. Every resulting class can be expressed, over $C'$, by homogeneous Satake morphisms, slides on neutral representations, and equivariant contractions. The transferred expressions respect tensoring by neutral spectators. When there is no Lie-variable factor, transfer identifies the holonomy matrix map with the same matrix map on the coherent side.
  - Explanation: Equivariant polynomial classes on the slice lift from global holonomy and Lie polynomials degree by degree. Homogeneous Satake maps, neutral slides, and contractions represent them while preserving spectator tensoring and holonomy matrices.
- **claim-spherical-line-reconstruction** (construction, [source](#stmt-local-spherical-reconstruction)): After shrinking the saturated slice $U_H$ around the identity class, there are an isomorphism $$\label{eq:spherical-line} L_S\simeq\mathcal O_Y$$ and an $H$-equivariant isomorphism of graded algebras over $\mathcal O(U_H)$ $$\label{eq:spherical-end} \operatorname{sh}H^\bullet(B_{\rm sph}^0) \xrightarrow{\ \sim\ } \underline{\operatorname{End}}^{\bullet}_{Y}(L_S) =\mathcal O(Y).$$ The isomorphism is induced by spherical transfer, duality, and $G_0$. It respects the representation-valued operations of Proposition <a href="#prop:trace-model" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:trace-model">26</a>. As an isomorphism between the two equation models over $U_H$, it preserves the open orbit and its boundary over every unipotent $u\in U_H$.
  - Explanation: After saturated shrinking, the spherical retract is an equivariantly trivial line and its internal endomorphism algebra is the positive equation ring. The comparison retains representation-valued operations and preserves open-orbit and boundary membership over unipotent holonomy.
- **claim-local-test-model-and-products** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-local-test-model)): Let $t$, $H=Z_L(t)$ and $m$ be associated with a selected place as in Lemmas <a href="#lem:test-places" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:test-places">9</a> and <a href="#lem:orbit-open" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:orbit-open">10</a>. After an etale base change on invariant holonomy functions and a saturated shrinking about the identity class, the Iwahori trace has a fully faithful coherent realization. Its composite with coherent duality, the covariant Koszul functor $G_0$, and the change of degree by $m$-weight is contravariant and has the following properties.* *Its unit corresponds to $$P=\bigoplus_bP_b\in\operatorname{Coh}^H(Y),\qquad Y=\{(u,e)\in U_H\times E_+:\operatorname{Ad}(u)e=e\},$$ where the $P_b$ are the derived flag direct images in <a href="#eq:flag-test" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:flag-test">[eq:flag-test]</a>. The scheme $Y$ is a classical complete intersection in the smooth affine scheme $U_H\times E_+$. The hyperspecial unit is the retract $B=\mathcal O_Y$ of $P$ under <a href="#eq:spherical-line" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:spherical-line">[eq:spherical-line]</a>. The unsheared representation comparisons are <a href="#eq:hom-comparison" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:hom-comparison">[eq:hom-comparison]</a>; their internal algebra and scalar actions are <a href="#eq:spherical-end" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:spherical-end">[eq:spherical-end]</a>. Before duality the slide on a label $V$ is the matrix $V(tu)$.* *The ordinary $H$-equivariant algebra $\operatorname{End}(P)^{\rm op}$ is the affine Hecke algebra with parameter $r$, pulled back along the etale map on its center. It has no higher cohomology, and its spherical corner is $\operatorname{End}(B)=\mathcal O(U_H)^H$.* *All these comparisons respect the matrix operations, homogeneous spherical maps, and neutral spectators of Proposition <a href="#prop:trace-model" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:trace-model">26</a>. They permit tests by every finite representation of $H/Z(L)$. In the even sectors used below, a test of $m$-weight $w$ changes cohomological degree from $b$ to $b+w$; the other sectors use the stated parity adjustment. The ring comparisons preserve the open orbit and boundary over unipotent $u$. The covariant coherent and Koszul realizations extend fully faithfully to Ind-completions; duality is applied to the compact test objects.* *The same statements hold for finite products of selected places, with external test objects, separate parity sectors, and the sum of the $m$-weights.
  - Explanation: The local comparisons combine into a fully faithful coherent test model with flag unit, structure-sheaf spherical retract, ordinary Hecke endomorphisms, and controlled weight regrading. The same construction applies to finite products in the external-test subcategory and its Ind-completion.
- **claim-trace-disjoint-hecke-agreement** (theorem, [source](#stmt-bounds-disjoint-hecke)): At a place carrying a unit path label, the spherical trace action on $\mathsf K_I$ agrees with the usual disjoint Hecke action on compact shtuka cohomology, with the Satake conventions fixed in Section <a href="#sec:global-support" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:global-support">2</a>. This agreement holds with arbitrary labels and full or parahoric levels at the other places.
  - Explanation: At a unit-labeled place, trace Hecke operations and the usual disjoint shtuka Hecke correspondences agree, retaining other labels and level structures.
- **claim-separating-curve-morphism** (construction, [source](#stmt-bounds-curve-map)): For disjoint finite sets $T_I,\operatorname{supp}(D),\{y_0\}\subset\|X\|$, there is a finite generically separable morphism $$\pi:X\longrightarrow\mathbb P^1_{\mathbb F_q}$$ unramified over $0,1,\infty$, such that $T_I$ lies above $0$, $\operatorname{supp}(D)$ lies above $\infty$, and $y_0$ lies above $1$.
  - Explanation: A finite generically separable curve morphism separates the selected Iwahori places, full-level support, and auxiliary Hecke place into the prescribed unramified fibers.
- **claim-simultaneous-nearby-comparison** (theorem, [source](#stmt-bounds-nearby)): For any finite $T_I$ and neutral labels $V_i$, the groups $N_b(V)$ are finitely generated $C$-modules. There is a finite set of additional places which can be omitted from the away Hecke actions such that the following comparison holds. For a sufficiently divisible $D_*$, $N_b(V)$ identifies, compatibly with $C$, the remaining away Hecke actions, and complete Frobenius, with a direct summand of moving-leg cohomology at the same level over a geometric generic tuple in $(U')^n$. On the latter group complete Frobenius acts through a tuple in $(\Gamma')^n$ whose degree is $D_*$ in every factor.
  - Explanation: Central-label path cohomology is Hecke-finitely generated and occurs as a direct summand of moving-leg cohomology after omitting finitely many away places. The comparison retains complete Frobenius with equal degree in each factor.
- **claim-localized-constituent-inclusion** (theorem, [source](#stmt-bounds-constituent-inclusion)): Every irreducible constituent of $D_b$ occurs among the constituents of $$\boxtimes_{i=1}^n\bigl(V_i\circ\sigma_{\nu,\mathrm{ad}}\bigr) \bigm\|_{(\Gamma')^n}. \label{eq:constituents}$$ This assertion concerns occurrence of constituents and does not prescribe their multiplicities.
  - Explanation: After imposing the selected Hecke values, every irreducible moving-leg constituent occurs in the external tensor representation of the given adjoint parameter. This controls occurrence without asserting multiplicities.
- **claim-localized-complete-frobenius-bound** (bound, [source](#stmt-bounds-weight-bound)): Quotient $N_b(V)$ by $\mathfrak m_C$ and localize at the neutral spherical Hecke values of $f$ outside the finite exceptional set in Proposition <a href="#bounds:nearby" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="bounds:nearby">37</a>. On the resulting finite-dimensional space, every eigenvalue $\alpha$ of complete Frobenius through a sufficiently divisible degree $D_*$ satisfies $$\|\alpha\|\leq q^{D_*b/2}. \label{bounds:frobenius-weight}$$
  - Explanation: After the stated Hecke quotient and localization, complete Frobenius eigenvalues satisfy the compact-cohomology upper bound through a sufficiently divisible degree. Constituent purity transfers the bound from disjoint good fibers to the local lifts.
- **claim-coherent-central-localization** (theorem, [source](#stmt-bounds-central-actions)): The algebra $\mathcal Z$ acts on the representing Ind-object by commuting dg endomorphisms. Pullback to the slices and localization at $\mathfrak m$ commute with mapping from compact objects. In particular, the latter operation computes ordinary flat localization on the cohomology of the mapping complexes.
  - Explanation: The filtered central algebra acts by commuting enhanced endomorphisms. Slice pullback and central localization commute with compact-source mapping and compute ordinary flat localization on mapping cohomology.
- **claim-uniform-simultaneous-lower-bound** (bound, [source](#stmt-thm-simultaneous-bound)): For every finite collection of the chosen places and every finite algebraic representation $W$ of $H^\natural$, one has $$\operatorname{RHom}(P\otimes W,F^{\mathrm{ind}})\in D^{\geq0}(k). \label{eq:lower-bound}$$ The lower bound zero is independent of the collection of places and of $W$.
  - Explanation: All finite selected-place collections and finite representation tests have nonnegative sheared mapping cohomology. The bound is independent of the collection and labels, even though the Frobenius power used to establish it may vary.
- **claim-ind-to-quasicoherent-test-comparison** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-ind-qc-comparison)): The complex $F^{\mathrm{qc}}$ is concentrated in degrees at least zero. If $Q$ belongs to the thick subcategory generated by the objects $P\otimes W$, for finite representations $W$ of $H^\natural$, then the natural comparison is an isomorphism: $$\operatorname{RHom}(Q,F^{\mathrm{ind}}) \xrightarrow{\ \sim\ }\operatorname{RHom}(Q,F^{\mathrm{qc}}). \label{eq:ind-qc-comparison}$$
  - Explanation: The ordinary quasi-coherent realization is nonnegative, and passage from the Ind-coherent object preserves maps from the thick closure of the flag test and its finite representation twists.
- **claim-boundary-cyclic-module-evaluation** (construction, [source](#stmt-boundary-cyclic-module)): There are morphisms $$\label{boundary:evaluation} \lambda_i:[\operatorname{Spec}R/A]\longrightarrow[Y_i/H_i^\natural]$$ whose field-valued images have unipotent residual holonomy and a vector in $E_i$ outside its open $H_i$-orbit. For every $n\geq1$, the product morphism $\lambda=(\lambda_1,\ldots,\lambda_n)$ is affine. Write $R_n$ for the $H^\natural$-equivariant $B$-algebra representing $\lambda_*\mathcal O$ on $Y$. There is an equivariant $B$-module map $$\label{eq:cyclic-module-map} R_n\longrightarrow H^0(F_n^{\mathrm{qc}})$$ that sends $1$ to the class of $f$ in the spherical summand. In particular, the composite $$\label{boundary:section-f} B\longrightarrow R_n\longrightarrow H^0(F_n^{\mathrm{qc}}) \longrightarrow F_n^{\mathrm{qc}}$$ is the section defined by $f$.
  - Explanation: Under the standing nonenhancement assumption, global support evaluates affinely in products of local models with unipotent residual holonomy and boundary vectors. Its equivariant module maps to nonnegative cohomology with the unit sent to the cuspidal class.
- **claim-bad-flag-boundary-detection** (theorem, [source](#stmt-boundary-bad-flag)): Let $(u,e)\in Y$ be a field point with $u$ unipotent and $e$ in the boundary of the open $H$-orbit in $E_+$. After extension of its residue field, there is a flag $\mathcal B$ of bad type such that $u\in\mathcal B\cap H$ and $e\in E_+\cap\mathfrak n_{\mathcal B}$.
  - Explanation: Every unipotent boundary pair admits a bad flag after residue-field extension. The flag contains the residual holonomy and places the raising vector in its nilradical.
- **claim-boundary-test-residue-fiber-vanishing** (theorem, [source](#stmt-boundary-fiber-zero)): The pullback of $q_i$ to $\operatorname{Spec}R$ is zero on every derived residue-field fiber.
  - Explanation: Bad-flag evaluation splits the incidence unit on each derived residue-field fiber over the global support. Consequently the fiber arrow of the local boundary test vanishes.
- **claim-successive-tensor-vanishing** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-tensor-nilpotence)): Let $S$ be a noetherian ring and let $v_i:D_i\to S$, $i\geq1$, be maps in $D(S)$ whose sources are pseudo-coherent and bounded above. Suppose $$v_i\otimes_S^{\mathbf L}\kappa(\mathfrak p)=0 \quad\text{for every }i\geq1\text{ and every }\mathfrak p\in\operatorname{Spec}S.$$ Then, for some $n\geq1$, $$\label{boundary:tensor-zero} v_1\otimes_S^{\mathbf L}\cdots\otimes_S^{\mathbf L}v_n=0.$$ More generally, for every bounded coherent complex $K$ and every starting index $a$, there is $b\geq a$ such that $(v_a\otimes^{\mathbf L}\cdots\otimes^{\mathbf L}v_b) \otimes^{\mathbf L}\operatorname{id}_K=0$.
  - Explanation: For a sequence of residue-field-zero maps over a noetherian ring with pseudo-coherent bounded-above sources, a finite initial tensor product vanishes. The parameter version works for every bounded coherent complex and every starting index, without giving an effective stopping bound.
- **claim-simple-hecke-quotient-detection** (theorem, [source](#stmt-boundary-simple-quotient)): The complex $\operatorname{RHom}(P,F_n^{\mathrm{ind}})$ is concentrated in degree zero. Its degree-zero module has a simple $\mathcal H$-quotient that detects $f$ in the spherical summand and on which the local centers act by the chosen classes of the $t_i$. In this simple quotient, every idempotent projecting to a bad flag summand in any one factor acts by zero.
  - Explanation: The Iwahori mapping module is concentrated in ordinary degree and has a simple quotient detecting the cuspidal spherical class at the selected central values. Every bad-flag projector annihilates that quotient.
- **claim-cuspidal-class-survives-boundary-tests** (theorem, [source](#stmt-boundary-f-survives)): For every finite initial segment of the selected places, $$q_\Pi^*f\ne0\quad\text{in } H^0\operatorname{RHom}(Q_\Pi,F_n^{\mathrm{ind}}).$$
  - Explanation: The cuspidal class survives every finite initial product of boundary tests. A simple Hecke quotient detects its spherical class while annihilating the images from bad factors, providing the nonvanishing needed for the final contradiction.

```json
          "exact_quote": "The morphism <a href=\"#eq:degree-two\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:degree-two\">[eq:degree-two]</a> induces an equivariant map $$E_b:M_b^{\\mathrm{reg}}\\longrightarrow\n                 M_{b+2}^{\\mathrm{reg}}\\otimes\\mathfrak g.$$ Its coordinates commute with each other and with the $\\mathscr B_\\Gamma$-action. Writing $\\mathbf e$ for the resulting Lie-algebra coordinate, so that its linear functions have degree two, one has the identity of operators $$\\label{eq:global-relation}\n           \\operatorname{Ad}(\\mathbf b(w))\\mathbf e\n                         =q^{\\deg(w)}\\mathbf e\n          \\qquad(w\\in\\Gamma).$$ These operations commute with all good-place Hecke actions.",
          "source_id": "PM-014.1"
        }
      ],
      "id": "claim-commuting-degree-two-coordinate",
      "kind": "construction",
      "quantifiers": [

          "exact_quote": "\\qquad(w\\in\\Gamma).",
          "source_id": "PM-014.1"
        }
      ],
      "statement_anchor": "stmt-lem-glob-degree-two"
    },
    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-glob-frames",
        "exact_quote": "As graded rational $A^n$-modules, $$\\label{eq:framed-module}\n \\mathcal M_n\\simeq\\operatorname{Ind}_A^{A^n}M_\\bullet^{\\mathrm{reg}}\n        =\\bigl(\\mathcal O(A^n)\\otimes M_\\bullet^{\\mathrm{reg}}\\bigr)^A.$$ If $g_i\\in A$ are the frame coordinates, with diagonal change of frame $g_i\\mapsto g_i h^{-1}$, then the adjoint holonomy and degree-two coordinates act as $$\\label{eq:framed-action}\n s_{i,\\mathrm{ad}}=g_i\\mathbf b(\\gamma_i)g_i^{-1},\n       \\qquad e_i=g_i\\mathbf e g_i^{-1}.$$",
        "source_id": "PM-014.1"
      },

          "source_id": "PM-014.1"
        }

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-thm-enhanced-iwahori",
        "exact_quote": "There is an enhanced monoidal equivalence $\\Theta:\\mathscr H\\simeq\\mathscr C$, together with a monoidal intertwining of $\\operatorname{Fr}^{d*}$ and $r_*$, having the following properties. There are identifications $$\\Theta Z(V)\\simeq D(V)\\qquad(V\\in\\operatorname{Rep}(L)),$$ natural for ordinary representation morphisms, and an element $z\\in Z(L)(k)$ such that the transported Weil isomorphism on $D(V)$ is its preferred isomorphism multiplied by $V(z)$. Under these identifications, the tensor maps between the $Z(V)$ become constant $L$-equivariant representation maps. On $\\operatorname{Rep}(A)\\subset\\operatorname{Rep}(L)$ they can be chosen to be the ordinary tensor maps. Finally, the central interchange of $Z(V)$ with $Z(W)$ becomes the standard interchange whenever at least one of $V,W$ is neutral.",
        "source_id": "PM-014.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-thm-enhanced-iwahori",
          "exact_quote": "There is an enhanced monoidal equivalence $\\Theta:\\mathscr H\\simeq\\mathscr C$, together with a monoidal intertwining of $\\operatorname{Fr}^{d*}$ and $r_*$, having the following properties. There are identifications $$\\Theta Z(V)\\simeq D(V)\\qquad(V\\in\\operatorname{Rep}(L)),$$ natural for ordinary representation morphisms, and an element $z\\in Z(L)(k)$ such that the transported Weil isomorphism on $D(V)$ is its preferred isomorphism multiplied by $V(z)$. Under these identifications, the tensor maps between the $Z(V)$ become constant $L$-equivariant representation maps. On $\\operatorname{Rep}(A)\\subset\\operatorname{Rep}(L)$ they can be chosen to be the ordinary tensor maps. Finally, the central interchange of $Z(V)$ with $Z(W)$ becomes the standard interchange whenever at least one of $V,W$ is neutral.",
          "source_id": "PM-014.1"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "There is an enhanced monoidal equivalence $\\Theta:\\mathscr H\\simeq\\mathscr C$, together with a monoidal intertwining of $\\operatorname{Fr}^{d*}$ and $r_*$, having the following properties. Under these identifications, the tensor maps between the $Z(V)$ become constant $L$-equivariant representation maps. On $\\operatorname{Rep}(A)\\subset\\operatorname{Rep}(L)$ they can be chosen to be the ordinary tensor maps. Finally, the central interchange of $Z(V)$ with $Z(W)$ becomes the standard interchange whenever at least one of $V,W$ is neutral.",

        "anchor": "stmt-enh-pure-formality",
        "exact_quote": "In the derived category of complexes over $k$ with one automorphism, consider the full subcategory consisting of complexes with bounded below, degreewise finite-dimensional cohomology, pure of weight $j$ in degree $j$. Its mapping spaces are discrete, and cohomology is an equivalence from this subcategory to the category of graded pure modules. This equivalence is symmetric monoidal for tensor over $k$ with the diagonal automorphism. Consequently, a diagram of mapping complexes of this kind, including all its multilinear composition and tensor operations, is determined by its cohomology diagram.",
        "source_id": "PM-014.1"
      },
      "evidence": [

          "source_id": "PM-014.1"

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-trace-coend",
        "exact_quote": "For a rigid enhanced kernel category with monoidal automorphism $\\Phi$, the images of kernels compactly generate its presentable trace and generate its horizontal trace under finite colimits and retracts. Moreover, $$\\label{eq:horizontal-trace}\n \\operatorname{Map}([J],[J'])\n   \\simeq \\int^{T}\\operatorname{Map}(\\Phi T*J,J'*T).$$ The formula applies to the matrix category with levels $I,K$. Each corner trace embeds fully faithfully in the matrix trace. At a closed point of degree $d$, the cyclic trace of its $d$ geometric factors is identified with the one-factor trace twisted by $\\operatorname{Fr}^{d*}$. This identification respects level transfers and full-cycle slides.",
        "source_id": "PM-014.1"
      },

At a closed point of degree $d$, the cyclic trace of its $d$ geometric factors is identified with the one-factor trace twisted by $\\operatorname{Fr}^{d*}$. This identification respects level transfers and full-cycle slides.",
          "source_id": "PM-014.1"
        }
      ],

        "exact_quote": "The equivalence of Theorem <a href=\"#thm:enhanced-iwahori\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"thm:enhanced-iwahori\">14</a> identifies the Iwahori horizontal trace with $\\operatorname{Coh}^L(Y_N)$, carrying $[\\mathbf1_I]$ to $P_N$ and $[Z(V)]$ to $P_N\\otimes V$. Sheaf duality $\\mathbb D=\\operatorname{RHom}(-,\\mathcal O)$ is a duality on this coherent model, and $\\mathbb DP_N\\simeq P_N$.*\n\n\n*Let $z\\in Z(L)$ be the element of Theorem <a href=\"#thm:enhanced-iwahori\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"thm:enhanced-iwahori\">14</a>. Full-cycle slide on the representation factor is $V(zs)$. Hyperspecial transfer identifies $[S(V)]$ with $P_N^S\\otimes V$, where $P_N^S$ is a retract of $P_N$. If $s_{\\rm sph}$ denotes spherical holonomy, then $$\\label{eq:coordinate-shift}\n s_{\\rm sph}=zs.$$ For neutral labels this comparison respects representation morphisms, homogeneous spherical-kernel morphisms, and matrix operations obtained from slides by composition, contraction, and tensoring by neutral spectators. It also respects invariant holonomy functions coming from arbitrary representations of $L$.",

      },

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-local-semisimple-slice",
        "exact_quote": "There is an affine saturated $H$-invariant open neighborhood $U_H\\subset H$ of the identity class such that the conjugation map $$\\label{local:eq-slice-map}\n L\\mathbin{\\times}^{H}(tU_H)\\longrightarrow L,\n \\qquad [g,tu]\\longmapsto gtu g^{-1},$$ is strongly étale over its image. More precisely, it is the pullback of the induced étale map $U_H/\\!/H\\to L/\\!/L$ along the adjoint quotient. The analogous statement holds with $t$ replaced by $z^{-1}t$. The neighborhood can be chosen so that the transverse conjugation operator and all nonresonant blocks of the vector equations are invertible. It contains the entire unipotent locus of $H$. The identity point of $U_H/\\!/H$ is the only point above the selected semisimple class; the corresponding fiber in $U_H$ still contains every unipotent element.",
        "source_id": "PM-014.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-local-semisimple-slice",
          "exact_quote": "There is an affine saturated $H$-invariant open neighborhood $U_H\\subset H$ of the identity class such that the conjugation map $$\\label{local:eq-slice-map}\n L\\mathbin{\\times}^{H}(tU_H)\\longrightarrow L,\n \\qquad [g,tu]\\longmapsto gtu g^{-1},$$ is strongly étale over its image. More precisely, it is the pullback of the induced étale map $U_H/\\!/H\\to L/\\!/L$ along the adjoint quotient. The analogous statement holds with $t$ replaced by $z^{-1}t$. The neighborhood can be chosen so that the transverse conjugation operator and all nonresonant blocks of the vector equations are invertible. It contains the entire unipotent locus of $H$. The identity point of $U_H/\\!/H$ is the only point above the selected semisimple class; the corresponding fiber in $U_H$ still contains every unipotent element.",
          "source_id": "PM-014.1"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "There is an affine saturated $H$-invariant open neighborhood $U_H\\subset H$ of the identity class such that the conjugation map $$\\label{local:eq-slice-map}\n L\\mathbin{\\times}^{H}(tU_H)\\longrightarrow L,\n \\qquad [g,tu]\\longmapsto gtu g^{-1},$$ is strongly étale over its image.       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-local-polynomial-representatives",
        "exact_quote": "Put $C=\\mathcal O(L)^L$ and $C'=\\mathcal O(U_H)^H$, with the map $C\\to C'$ given by the spherical coordinate $s_{\\rm sph}=tu$. Write $B_{\\rm sph}=\\operatorname{sh}H^\\bullet(B_{\\rm sph}^0)$ for the classical spherical equation algebra, retaining polynomial degree as the original cohomological degree divided by two. For neutral representations $V,V'$ the restriction and quotient map $$\\label{local:eq-polynomial-surjection}\n \\bigl(C'\\otimes_C\n    (\\mathcal O(L)\\otimes\\operatorname{Sym}(\\mathfrak g^*)\\otimes V^*\\otimes V')\\bigr)^L\n \\longrightarrow\n (B_{\\rm sph}\\otimes V^*\\otimes V')^H$$ is surjective and preserves polynomial degree. Every resulting class can be expressed, over $C'$, by homogeneous Satake morphisms, slides on neutral representations, and equivariant contractions. The transferred expressions respect tensoring by neutral spectators. When there is no Lie-variable factor, transfer identifies the holonomy matrix map with the same matrix map on the coherent side.",
        "source_id": "PM-014.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-local-polynomial-representatives",

Every resulting class can be expressed, over $C'$, by homogeneous Satake morphisms, slides on neutral representations, and equivariant contractions.       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-local-spherical-reconstruction",
        "exact_quote": "After shrinking the saturated slice $U_H$ around the identity class, there are an isomorphism $$\\label{eq:spherical-line}\n L_S\\simeq\\mathcal O_Y$$ and an $H$-equivariant isomorphism of graded algebras over $\\mathcal O(U_H)$ $$\\label{eq:spherical-end}\n \\operatorname{sh}H^\\bullet(B_{\\rm sph}^0)\n   \\xrightarrow{\\ \\sim\\ }\n \\underline{\\operatorname{End}}^{\\bullet}_{Y}(L_S)\n   =\\mathcal O(Y).$$ The isomorphism is induced by spherical transfer, duality, and $G_0$. It respects the representation-valued operations of Proposition <a href=\"#prop:trace-model\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"prop:trace-model\">26</a>.       "evidence": [

      "exact_statement": "After shrinking the saturated slice $U_H$ around the identity class, there are an isomorphism $$\\label{eq:spherical-line}\n L_S\\simeq\\mathcal O_Y$$ and an $H$-equivariant isomorphism of graded algebras over $\\mathcal O(U_H)$ $$\\label{eq:spherical-end}\n \\operatorname{sh}H^\\bullet(B_{\\rm sph}^0)\n   \\xrightarrow{\\ \\sim\\ }\n \\underline{\\operatorname{End}}^{\\bullet}_{Y}(L_S)\n   =\\mathcal O(Y).$$ The isomorphism is induced by spherical transfer, duality, and $G_0$. It respects the representation-valued operations of Proposition <a href=\"#prop:trace-model\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"prop:trace-model\">26</a>.       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-thm-local-test-model",
        "exact_quote": "Let $t$, $H=Z_L(t)$ and $m$ be associated with a selected place as in Lemmas <a href=\"#lem:test-places\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"lem:test-places\">9</a> and <a href=\"#lem:orbit-open\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"lem:orbit-open\">10</a>. After an etale base change on invariant holonomy functions and a saturated shrinking about the identity class, the Iwahori trace has a fully faithful coherent realization. Its composite with coherent duality, the covariant Koszul functor $G_0$, and the change of degree by $m$-weight is contravariant and has the following properties.*\n\n*Its unit corresponds to $$P=\\bigoplus_bP_b\\in\\operatorname{Coh}^H(Y),\\qquad\n Y=\\{(u,e)\\in U_H\\times E_+:\\operatorname{Ad}(u)e=e\\},$$ where the $P_b$ are the derived flag direct images in <a href=\"#eq:flag-test\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:flag-test\">[eq:flag-test]</a>. The scheme $Y$ is a classical complete intersection in the smooth affine scheme $U_H\\times E_+$. The hyperspecial unit is the retract $B=\\mathcal O_Y$ of $P$ under <a href=\"#eq:spherical-line\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:spherical-line\">[eq:spherical-line]</a>. The unsheared representation comparisons are <a href=\"#eq:hom-comparison\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:hom-comparison\">[eq:hom-comparison]</a>; their internal algebra and scalar actions are <a href=\"#eq:spherical-end\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:spherical-end\">[eq:spherical-end]</a>. Before duality the slide on a label $V$ is the matrix $V(tu)$.*\n\n*The ordinary $H$-equivariant algebra $\\operatorname{End}(P)^{\\rm op}$ is the affine Hecke algebra with parameter $r$, pulled back along the etale map on its center. It has no higher cohomology, and its spherical corner is $\\operatorname{End}(B)=\\mathcal O(U_H)^H$.*\n\n*All these comparisons respect the matrix operations, homogeneous spherical maps, and neutral spectators of Proposition <a href=\"#prop:trace-model\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"prop:trace-model\">26</a>. They permit tests by every finite representation of $H/Z(L)$. In the even sectors used below, a test of $m$-weight $w$ changes cohomological degree from $b$ to $b+w$; the other sectors use the stated parity adjustment. The ring comparisons preserve the open orbit and boundary over unipotent $u$. The covariant coherent and Koszul realizations extend fully faithfully to Ind-completions; duality is applied to the compact test objects.*\n\n*The same statements hold for finite products of selected places, with external test objects, separate parity sectors, and the sum of the $m$-weights.",

      },

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-thm-simultaneous-bound",
        "exact_quote": "For every finite collection of the chosen places and every finite algebraic representation $W$ of $H^\\natural$, one has $$\\operatorname{RHom}(P\\otimes W,F^{\\mathrm{ind}})\\in D^{\\geq0}(k).\n \\label{eq:lower-bound}$$ The lower bound zero is independent of the collection of places and of $W$.",

        "anchor": "stmt-boundary-cyclic-module",
        "exact_quote": "There are morphisms $$\\label{boundary:evaluation}\n \\lambda_i:[\\operatorname{Spec}R/A]\\longrightarrow[Y_i/H_i^\\natural]$$ whose field-valued images have unipotent residual holonomy and a vector in $E_i$ outside its open $H_i$-orbit. For every $n\\geq1$, the product morphism $\\lambda=(\\lambda_1,\\ldots,\\lambda_n)$ is affine. Write $R_n$ for the $H^\\natural$-equivariant $B$-algebra representing $\\lambda_*\\mathcal O$ on $Y$. There is an equivariant $B$-module map $$\\label{eq:cyclic-module-map}\n R_n\\longrightarrow H^0(F_n^{\\mathrm{qc}})$$ that sends $1$ to the class of $f$ in the spherical summand. In particular, the composite $$\\label{boundary:section-f}\n B\\longrightarrow R_n\\longrightarrow H^0(F_n^{\\mathrm{qc}})\n       \\longrightarrow F_n^{\\mathrm{qc}}$$ is the section defined by $f$.",
        "source_id": "PM-014.1"
      },

      ],
      "exact_statement": "There are morphisms $$\\label{boundary:evaluation}\n \\lambda_i:[\\operatorname{Spec}R/A]\\longrightarrow[Y_i/H_i^\\natural]$$ whose field-valued images have unipotent residual holonomy and a vector in $E_i$ outside its open $H_i$-orbit. For every $n\\geq1$, the product morphism $\\lambda=(\\lambda_1,\\ldots,\\lambda_n)$ is affine. Write $R_n$ for the $H^\\natural$-equivariant $B$-algebra representing $\\lambda_*\\mathcal O$ on $Y$.       "explanation": "Under the standing nonenhancement assumption, global support evaluates affinely in products of local models with unipotent residual holonomy and boundary vectors. Its equivariant module maps to nonnegative cohomology with the unit sent to the cuspidal class.",
      "fidelity": "PENDING",
      "hypotheses": [
        {
          "anchor": "sec:boundary",
          "exact_quote": "### Simultaneous boundary tests and completion of the proof We now compare the two consequences of a failure of enhancement. By Proposition <a href=\"#prop:open-orbit-enhancement\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"prop:open-orbit-enhancement\">12</a>, such a failure confines the finite type support $\\operatorname{Spec}R$ to the boundary of the open orbit at every selected place. We shall construct a coherent test at each place whose map to the spherical summand vanishes on every derived fiber over this support. Noetherianity then makes a finite product of these maps vanish on the whole support. The cuspidal vector $f$, however, survives every such product, as we shall see from a simple quotient of its Iwahori Hecke module. Throughout this section, assume that the occurring excursion character $\\nu$ has no enhancement as in Theorem <a href=\"#thm:main\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"thm:main\">2</a>. Retain the nonzero eigenvector $f$ and its cyclic support algebra $R$ from Proposition <a href=\"#prop:global-support\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"prop:global-support\">8</a>, together with the infinite sequence of places $x_i$ from Lemma <a href=\"#lem:test-places\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"lem:test-places\">9</a>. At $x_i$ write $$r_i=q^{\\deg x_i},\\qquad H_i=Z_L(t_i),\\qquad E_i=\\ker(\\operatorname{Ad}(t_i)-r_i),\\qquad H_i^\\natural=H_i/Z(L).$$ Let $m_i$ be the cocharacter of the prescribed triple. The local models of Theorem <a href=\"#thm:local-test-model\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"thm:local-test-model\">34</a> are $$Y_i=\\{(u,e)\\in U_{H_i}\\times E_i:\\operatorname{Ad}(u)e=e\\},\\qquad B_i=\\mathcal O(Y_i),\\qquad P_i=\\bigoplus_b P_{i,b}.$$ Here $b$ runs through the components of the $t_i$-fixed flag variety, and $P_{i,b}$ is the derived incidence pushforward in <a href=\"#eq:flag-test\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:flag-test\">[eq:flag-test]</a>. The spherical summand of $P_i$ is $B_i$. For a finite initial segment of length $n$, put $$Y=\\prod_{i=1}^nY_i,\\qquad B=\\bigotimes_{i=1}^nB_i,\\qquad P=\\boxtimes_{i=1}^nP_i,\\qquad H^\\natural=\\prod_{i=1}^nH_i^\\natural.$$ We use $F_n^{\\mathrm{ind}}$ and $F_n^{\\mathrm{qc}}$ for the localized, sheared objects constructed in Section <a href=\"#sec:lower-bound\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"sec:lower-bound\">7</a>. Thus $F_n^{\\mathrm{qc}}\\in D^{\\geq0}\\operatorname{QCoh}^{H^\\natural}(Y)$, and Proposition <a href=\"#prop:ind-qc-comparison\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"prop:ind-qc-comparison\">42</a> compares mapping complexes from the thick closure of $P$, including its representation twists.",
          "source_id": "PM-014.1"
        }
      ],

          "anchor": "sec:boundary",
          "exact_quote": "### Simultaneous boundary tests and completion of the proof We now compare the two consequences of a failure of enhancement. By Proposition <a href=\"#prop:open-orbit-enhancement\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"prop:open-orbit-enhancement\">12</a>, such a failure confines the finite type support $\\operatorname{Spec}R$ to the boundary of the open orbit at every selected place. We shall construct a coherent test at each place whose map to the spherical summand vanishes on every derived fiber over this support. Noetherianity then makes a finite product of these maps vanish on the whole support. The cuspidal vector $f$, however, survives every such product, as we shall see from a simple quotient of its Iwahori Hecke module. Throughout this section, assume that the occurring excursion character $\\nu$ has no enhancement as in Theorem <a href=\"#thm:main\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"thm:main\">2</a>. Retain the nonzero eigenvector $f$ and its cyclic support algebra $R$ from Proposition <a href=\"#prop:global-support\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"prop:global-support\">8</a>, together with the infinite sequence of places $x_i$ from Lemma <a href=\"#lem:test-places\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"lem:test-places\">9</a>. At $x_i$ write $$r_i=q^{\\deg x_i},\\qquad H_i=Z_L(t_i),\\qquad E_i=\\ker(\\operatorname{Ad}(t_i)-r_i),\\qquad H_i^\\natural=H_i/Z(L).$$ Let $m_i$ be the cocharacter of the prescribed triple. The local models of Theorem <a href=\"#thm:local-test-model\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"thm:local-test-model\">34</a> are $$Y_i=\\{(u,e)\\in U_{H_i}\\times E_i:\\operatorname{Ad}(u)e=e\\},\\qquad B_i=\\mathcal O(Y_i),\\qquad P_i=\\bigoplus_b P_{i,b}.$$ Here $b$ runs through the components of the $t_i$-fixed flag variety, and $P_{i,b}$ is the derived incidence pushforward in <a href=\"#eq:flag-test\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:flag-test\">[eq:flag-test]</a>. The spherical summand of $P_i$ is $B_i$. For a finite initial segment of length $n$, put $$Y=\\prod_{i=1}^nY_i,\\qquad B=\\bigotimes_{i=1}^nB_i,\\qquad P=\\boxtimes_{i=1}^nP_i,\\qquad H^\\natural=\\prod_{i=1}^nH_i^\\natural.$$ We use $F_n^{\\mathrm{ind}}$ and $F_n^{\\mathrm{qc}}$ for the localized, sheared objects constructed in Section <a href=\"#sec:lower-bound\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"sec:lower-bound\">7</a>. Thus $F_n^{\\mathrm{qc}}\\in D^{\\geq0}\\operatorname{QCoh}^{H^\\natural}(Y)$, and Proposition <a href=\"#prop:ind-qc-comparison\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"prop:ind-qc-comparison\">42</a> compares mapping complexes from the thick closure of $P$, including its representation twists.",
          "source_id": "PM-014.1"
        }
      ],
      "id": "claim-cuspidal-class-survives-boundary-tests",
      "kind": "theorem",
      "quantifiers": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-boundary-f-survives",
          "exact_quote": "For every finite initial segment of the selected places,",
          "source_id": "PM-014.1"
        }
      ],
      "statement_anchor": "stmt-boundary-f-survives"
    }
  ],
  "generation_status": "COMPLETE",
  "pm_id": "PM-014.1",
  "schema": "pm.claims.v2"
}
```
<!-- PM:END pm.claims.v2 -->

<a id="pm-s2"></a>
## 2. Definitions, validity and bounds

- **Full finite level subgroup** ([source](#pm-h-3)): The full level subgroup is $$K_D=\ker\bigl(G(\mathcal O_{\mathbb A})\longrightarrow G(\mathcal O_D)\bigr).$$
- **Cuspidal function space** ([source](#pm-h-3)): Let $C_D$ be the $k$-space of compactly supported functions on $G(F)\backslash G(\mathbb A_F)/K_D$ whose constant terms along all proper $F$-parabolic subgroups vanish.
- **Occurring excursion character** ([source](#pm-h-3)): An *occurring excursion character* is a character $\nu:\mathcal B_D\to k$ with nonzero simultaneous generalized eigenspace.
- **Excursion parameter** ([source](#pm-h-3)): Its parameter is a conjugacy class of continuous homomorphisms $$\sigma_\nu:\pi_1(U)\longrightarrow L(k)$$ defined over a finite extension of $\mathbb Q_\ell$, with reductive Zariski closure.
- **Weil group of the good open** ([source](#pm-h-3)): Let $\Gamma=W_U$ be the inverse image of $\mathbb Z$ under $\pi_1(U)\to\operatorname{Gal}(\overline{\mathbb F}_q/\mathbb F_q)=\widehat\mathbb Z$, with its Weil topology.
- **Neutral representation** ([source](#pm-h-7)): A *neutral* representation of $L$ is one factoring through $A$.
- **Bounded coherent category convention** ([source](#pm-h-7)): The notation $\operatorname{Coh}$ means bounded derived coherent complexes, and shifts are cohomological.
- **Universal abstract representation algebra** ([source](#pm-h-10)): Let $\mathscr B_\Gamma$ be the coordinate algebra of the scheme $\operatorname{Hom}(\Gamma,A)$, with $\Gamma$ regarded as an abstract group.
- **Cyclic support algebra** ([source](#pm-h-12)): Define $$R=\mathscr R_\Gamma/\operatorname{Ann}(f).$$
- **IC convolution word** ([source](#pm-h-18)): A *word* will mean a convolution of finitely many such normalized IC complexes, including the empty word $1_I$.
- **Horizontal trace** ([source](#pm-h-23)): By its *horizontal trace* we mean the small idempotent-complete category of compact objects in this tensor product.
- **Shear of an even-weight module** ([source](#local:subsec-slice)): For an even-weight rational module $M=\bigoplus_w M_w$, its *shear* places $M_w^d$ in degree $d+w$.
- **Positive fixed-vector scheme** ([source](#local:subsec-slice)): Define the ordinary affine scheme $$\label{local:eq-positive-space} Y=\{(u,e)\in U_H\times E_+:\operatorname{Ad}(u)e=e\}.$$
- **Derived flag test object** ([source](#eq:flag-test)): Set $$\label{eq:flag-test} P_b=R\pi_{b*}\mathcal O_{\widetilde Y_b},\qquad P=\bigoplus_b P_b.$$
- **Bad flag type** ([source](#pm-h-39)): A component $b$ of the $t$-fixed flag variety is called *bad* if $$E_+\cap\mathfrak n_{\mathcal B}$$ contains no point of the open $H$-orbit in $E_+$, for a flag $\mathcal B$ of type $b$.
- **Boundary test fiber map** ([source](#eq:boundary-test)): For each place, let the units of the derived incidence algebras define $$\label{eq:boundary-test} q_i:Q_i\longrightarrow B_i, \qquad Q_i=\operatorname{fib}\left(B_i\longrightarrow \bigoplus_{b\ \mathrm{bad}}P_{i,b}\right).$$

| q-compact-cohomology-weight | Complete Frobenius weight | ≤ | $b$ | Distinct leg points rational over the specified finite extension; finite-dimensional subquotients. |

| q-trace-retraction-scalar | Hyperspecial transfer retraction scalar | = | $\#(K/I)(\mathbb F_{q^d})$ | A selected closed point and its full flag variety. |
| q-equation-scheme-dimension | Dimension of the classical resonance equation schemes | = | $\dim H$ | The connected centralizer at a selected place. |
| q-smooth-flag-base-dimension | Dimension of the sliced flag base | = | $\dim H$ | Each component of the fixed flag variety. |
| q-localized-frobenius-modulus | Absolute value of a complete Frobenius eigenvalue | ≤ | $q^{D_*b/2}$ | A sufficiently divisible complete Frobenius degree and the original cohomological degree. |
| q-sheared-mapping-lower-bound | Cohomological degree of the simultaneous representation test | ≥ | $0$ | All finite collections of selected places and finite algebraic representation labels. |
| q-quasicoherent-lower-bound | Cohomological degree of the ordinary quasi-coherent realization | ≥ | $0$ | The product of the selected local equation schemes. |
| q-iwahori-mapping-degree | Occupied cohomological degree of the Iwahori-unit mapping complex | = | $0$ | A finite initial segment of selected places. |

```json
            "anchor": "sec:boundary",
            "exact_quote": "### Simultaneous boundary tests and completion of the proof We now compare the two consequences of a failure of enhancement. By Proposition <a href=\"#prop:open-orbit-enhancement\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"prop:open-orbit-enhancement\">12</a>, such a failure confines the finite type support $\\operatorname{Spec}R$ to the boundary of the open orbit at every selected place. We shall construct a coherent test at each place whose map to the spherical summand vanishes on every derived fiber over this support. Noetherianity then makes a finite product of these maps vanish on the whole support. The cuspidal vector $f$, however, survives every such product, as we shall see from a simple quotient of its Iwahori Hecke module. Throughout this section, assume that the occurring excursion character $\\nu$ has no enhancement as in Theorem <a href=\"#thm:main\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"thm:main\">2</a>. Retain the nonzero eigenvector $f$ and its cyclic support algebra $R$ from Proposition <a href=\"#prop:global-support\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"prop:global-support\">8</a>, together with the infinite sequence of places $x_i$ from Lemma <a href=\"#lem:test-places\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"lem:test-places\">9</a>. At $x_i$ write $$r_i=q^{\\deg x_i},\\qquad H_i=Z_L(t_i),\\qquad E_i=\\ker(\\operatorname{Ad}(t_i)-r_i),\\qquad H_i^\\natural=H_i/Z(L).$$ Let $m_i$ be the cocharacter of the prescribed triple. The local models of Theorem <a href=\"#thm:local-test-model\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"thm:local-test-model\">34</a> are $$Y_i=\\{(u,e)\\in U_{H_i}\\times E_i:\\operatorname{Ad}(u)e=e\\},\\qquad B_i=\\mathcal O(Y_i),\\qquad P_i=\\bigoplus_b P_{i,b}.$$ Here $b$ runs through the components of the $t_i$-fixed flag variety, and $P_{i,b}$ is the derived incidence pushforward in <a href=\"#eq:flag-test\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:flag-test\">[eq:flag-test]</a>. The spherical summand of $P_i$ is $B_i$. For a finite initial segment of length $n$, put $$Y=\\prod_{i=1}^nY_i,\\qquad B=\\bigotimes_{i=1}^nB_i,\\qquad P=\\boxtimes_{i=1}^nP_i,\\qquad H^\\natural=\\prod_{i=1}^nH_i^\\natural.$$ We use $F_n^{\\mathrm{ind}}$ and $F_n^{\\mathrm{qc}}$ for the localized, sheared objects constructed in Section <a href=\"#sec:lower-bound\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"sec:lower-bound\">7</a>. Thus $F_n^{\\mathrm{qc}}\\in D^{\\geq0}\\operatorname{QCoh}^{H^\\natural}(Y)$, and Proposition <a href=\"#prop:ind-qc-comparison\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"prop:ind-qc-comparison\">42</a> compares mapping complexes from the thick closure of $P$, including its representation twists.",
```
