<a id="pm-s3"></a>
## 3. Proof roadmap

- **step-spectral-input** [claim](#stmt-thm-parent) / [proof](#stmt-thm-parent): Use the stated finite-level spectral theorem as an input; its proof is not supplied here. -> A common nilpotent orbit and compact local commuting factors.
- **step-cohomological-weil-actions** [claim](#stmt-lem-glob-cohomology) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-glob-cohomology): Combine shtuka finiteness, ind-smoothness, fusion, and finite-dimensional Weil quotients. -> Partial Weil actions and commuting Hecke operations with spectators.
- **step-compact-weight-bound** [claim](#stmt-lem-glob-weights) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-glob-weights): Apply mixed compact direct-image weights on bounded opens and pass to finite-dimensional subquotients. -> The complete Frobenius weight upper bound.
- **step-matrix-entry-algebra** [claim](#stmt-lem-glob-matrices) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-glob-matrices): Test separate legs, use fusion, and impose tensor and group-law relations. -> The equivariant universal representation-algebra action.
- **step-relative-degree-two-operation** [claim](#stmt-lem-glob-degree-two) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-glob-degree-two): Globalize the enhanced Satake morphism on the coordinate product and verify partial Frobenius compatibility before fusion. -> Commuting Lie coordinates with the global scaling equation.
- **step-frames-match-local-matrices** [claim](#stmt-lem-glob-frames) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-glob-frames): Use invariant representation tests, Frobenius reciprocity, and the full-cycle slide with spectators. -> Induced framed modules with matched holonomy and Lie coordinates.
- **step-finite-type-cyclic-support** [claim](#stmt-prop-global-support) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-global-support): Take the annihilator quotient of the eigenvector and use Hecke finite generation to bound the matrix-coordinate span. -> A nonzero finite-type support retaining all invariant tuple values.
- **step-select-connected-test-places** [claim](#stmt-lem-test-places) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-test-places): Use Chebotarev in a small identity congruence neighborhood and compare Weyl stabilizers through the torus logarithm. -> An infinite sequence with connected centralizers and the central-torsor property.
- **step-open-resonance-geometry** [claim](#stmt-lem-orbit-open) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-orbit-open): Analyze absolute-value cocharacter directions and the raising tangent map in triple representations. -> The unique open resonance orbit and its reductive stabilizer.
- **step-full-tuple-conjugacy** [claim](#stmt-lem-orbit-tuples) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-orbit-tuples): Enlarge tuples to closed-orbit tests and apply noetherianity to the closed conjugator conditions. -> A single conjugator for the entire abstract group.
- **step-reconstruct-open-orbit-enhancement** [claim](#stmt-prop-open-orbit-enhancement) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-open-orbit-enhancement): Project scaling normalizers to Levi factors, make the product closure reductive, recover the parameter by tuple conjugacy, lift through the center, and prove purity by orthogonality. -> The prescribed continuous commuting pair with reductive closure and purity.
- **step-nonenhancement-forces-boundary** [claim](#stmt-cor-orbit-boundary) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cor-orbit-boundary): Take the contrapositive of open-orbit reconstruction and use constructibility to pass from closed points to geometric points. -> Boundary confinement at every selected place.
- **step-normalize-frobenius-dilation** [claim](#stmt-enh-frobenius-normalization) / [proof](#proof-stmt-enh-frobenius-normalization): Track monodromic generator maps and derived module localization with full bar mapping complexes. -> The natural exact Frobenius–dilation intertwiner.
- **step-identify-central-objects** [claim](#stmt-enh-central-objects) / [proof](#proof-stmt-enh-central-objects): Compare after forgetting equivariance and use closed-immersion direct image on coherent hearts. -> Geometric diagonal representation kernels.
- **step-pure-word-mapping-modules** [claim](#stmt-enh-pure-homs) / [proof](#proof-stmt-enh-pure-homs): Use pure stalks and costalks to degenerate the local and orbit Hom spectral sequences. -> Pure, finitely generated, torsion-free equivariant Hom modules.
- **step-localize-standard-category** [claim](#stmt-enh-localized-standards) / [proof](#proof-stmt-enh-localized-standards): Invert homogeneous equivariant classes, eliminate simple-wall Euler cones, and decompose homogeneous matrices over the graded field. -> Orthogonal shifted standards and unlocalized component links.
- **step-remove-frobenius-tensor-defect** [claim](#stmt-enh-monoidal-frobenius) / [proof](#proof-stmt-enh-monoidal-frobenius): Reduce the tensor discrepancy to a component cocycle and remove its iterated class by a scalar cochain. -> A monoidal intertwiner on the complete graded word diagram.
- **step-pure-complex-formality** [claim](#stmt-enh-pure-formality) / [proof](#proof-stmt-enh-pure-formality): Split complexes over the hereditary automorphism ring and separate spectra of distinct weights. -> Discrete mapping spaces and a symmetric monoidal cohomology equivalence.
- **step-lift-enriched-word-diagram** [claim](#stmt-enh-lift) / [proof](#proof-stmt-enh-lift): Identify enriched multimorphism diagrams by purity and extend through stable idempotent-complete envelopes. -> The enhanced monoidal equivalence with Frobenius.
- **step-compare-mixed-central-filtrations** [claim](#stmt-enh-mixed-central-comparison) / [proof](#proof-stmt-enh-mixed-central-comparison): Extend pure-piece identifications through the weight filtration using negative-weight extension and Frobenius-defect groups. -> Weil-compatible agreement on the central objects.
- **step-normalize-central-tensor-data** [claim](#stmt-enh-central-normalization) / [proof](#proof-stmt-enh-central-normalization): Remove positive-degree Weil terms, recover central scalar actions, and normalize neutral symmetric tensor data. -> Constant tensor maps, a central Weil correction, and the specified standard interchanges.
- **step-complete-enhanced-iwahori-theorem** [claim](#stmt-thm-enhanced-iwahori) / [proof](#proof-stmt-enh-central-normalization-2): Combine the enhanced lift, mixed central comparison, and central normalization. -> The full enhanced Iwahori comparison with its stated scope.
- **step-derived-trace-coend-calculus** [claim](#stmt-trace-coend) / [proof](#proof-stmt-trace-coend): Use relative tensor products, rigidity, balancing, and successive derived coends. -> Trace mapping formulas, corner embeddings, and cyclic-factor contraction.
- **step-compute-universal-hecke-trace** [claim](#stmt-trace-hecke-algebra) / [proof](#proof-stmt-trace-hecke-algebra): Apply standard-stratum trace additivity and verify convolution relations through rank-one compact cohomology. -> The ordinary affine Hecke algebra with normalized generators.
- **step-coherent-trace-and-spherical-maps** [claim](#stmt-prop-trace-model) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-trace-model): Apply the coherent trace theorem, compute cut transport, and identify the spherical idempotent using projection transfers. -> The self-dual Springer model, spherical retract, and full map comparisons.
- **step-strong-semisimple-slice** [claim](#stmt-local-semisimple-slice) / [proof](#proof-stmt-local-semisimple-slice): Invert nonresonant determinant blocks and apply the strongly étale semisimple slice with saturated shrinking. -> An affine slice retaining all unipotent residual holonomy.
- **step-equation-complete-intersections** [claim](#stmt-local-equation-models) / [proof](#proof-stmt-local-equation-models): Compute stabilizer-pair dimensions orbit by orbit and compare with the number of equations. -> Classical complete-intersection equation models.
- **step-covariant-koszul-full-faithfulness** [claim](#stmt-local-graded-koszul) / [proof](#proof-stmt-local-graded-koszul): Resolve the augmentation, compute the rational dual algebra, prove coherent shear, and eliminate reconstruction cones through positive-weight localization. -> The fully faithful coherent Koszul functor and its Ind-extension.
- **step-slice-derived-flag-incidence** [claim](#stmt-local-flag-slice) / [proof](#proof-stmt-local-flag-slice): Identify the smooth group–flag incidence and eliminate nonresonant vector-equation pairs. -> Disjoint smooth flag bases with derived vector equations.
- **step-koszul-transform-flag-object** [claim](#stmt-local-flag-image) / [proof](#proof-stmt-local-flag-image): Use augmentation self-duality, cancel matching determinant factors, and commute the transform with proper flag direct image. -> The positive flag object without shift or line twist.
- **step-polynomial-matrix-lifts** [claim](#stmt-local-polynomial-representatives) / [proof](#proof-stmt-local-polynomial-representatives): Use flat slice base change, exact reductive invariants, and neutral matrix coefficients with homogeneous Satake operations. -> Degree-preserving polynomial representatives with spectator compatibility.
- **step-reconstruct-spherical-line** [claim](#stmt-local-spherical-reconstruction) / [proof](#proof-stmt-local-spherical-reconstruction): Recover the internal algebra from representation tests, prove the retract perfect of rank one, and trivialize it by an invariant section after shrinking. -> The structure-sheaf spherical object with boundary-preserving algebra comparison.
- **step-assemble-local-product-tests** [claim](#stmt-thm-local-test-model) / [proof](#proof-stmt-thm-local-test-model): Combine the local comparisons and tensor their cut, Hom, and coend calculations for external test objects. -> Fully faithful local and product coherent test models.
- **step-identify-disjoint-hecke-actions** [claim](#stmt-bounds-disjoint-hecke) / [proof](#proof-stmt-bounds-disjoint-hecke): Compare modification correspondences and Frobenius-fixed flag transfer with the prescribed Satake normalization. -> Agreement of trace and disjoint cohomological Hecke actions.
- **step-choose-simultaneous-degeneration-map** [claim](#stmt-bounds-curve-map) / [proof](#proof-stmt-bounds-curve-map): Prescribe first jets of ample-line-bundle sections and use the finite-field tail estimate to obtain simple fibers. -> A separating finite generically separable curve map.
- **step-simultaneous-nearby-finiteness** [claim](#stmt-bounds-nearby) / [proof](#proof-stmt-bounds-nearby): Use Weil restriction to a single degeneration, apply the one-leg nearby comparison, recover level by invariants, and transport ind-smooth fibers. -> Hecke finite generation and a Frobenius-compatible moving-leg summand.
- **step-control-localized-constituents** [claim](#stmt-bounds-constituent-inclusion) / [proof](#proof-stmt-bounds-constituent-inclusion): Specialize a finite-type matrix algebra, extend continuous representations, use Chebotarev, and kill a power of the constituent annihilator. -> Constituent occurrence inside the adjoint-parameter external tensor.
- **step-transfer-arithmetic-weight-bound** [claim](#stmt-bounds-weight-bound) / [proof](#proof-stmt-bounds-weight-bound): Use pure irreducible lisse constituents, impose the compact bound at disjoint good points, and transport to the local Frobenius lifts. -> The complete Frobenius modulus bound after Hecke reduction.
- **step-central-dg-actions-and-localization** [claim](#stmt-bounds-central-actions) / [proof](#proof-stmt-bounds-central-actions): Insert finite collections of unit labels coherently and form the filtered central action; map from compact sources through flat localization. -> Commuting enhanced central scalars and ordinary localization on mapping cohomology.
- **step-uniform-nonnegative-sheared-tests** [claim](#stmt-thm-simultaneous-bound) / [proof](#proof-stmt-thm-simultaneous-bound): Compare the dual-label Frobenius modulus with the arithmetic upper bound and apply Nakayama after central reduction. -> The place-independent nonnegative mapping bound.
- **step-pass-to-quasicoherent-tests** [claim](#stmt-prop-ind-qc-comparison) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-ind-qc-comparison): Detect ordinary cohomology using spherical representation tests and apply the regular-embedding conormal filtration to comparison cones. -> Nonnegative quasi-coherent realization and mapping comparison on the required thick closure.
- **step-place-cyclic-support-on-local-products** [claim](#stmt-boundary-cyclic-module) / [proof](#proof-stmt-boundary-cyclic-module): Evaluate universal matrices on slice sheets, descend framed affine presentations, and transport the cyclic module action through duality and shear. -> Affine evaluation maps and a module section carrying the unit to the cuspidal class.
- **step-find-bad-flags** [claim](#stmt-boundary-bad-flag) / [proof](#proof-stmt-boundary-bad-flag): Choose a compatible triple and a lexicographic Borel, then use the determinant semi-invariant to exclude open-orbit vectors from its type. -> A bad flag detecting the boundary pair.
- **step-split-boundary-units-on-fibers** [claim](#stmt-boundary-fiber-zero) / [proof](#proof-stmt-boundary-fiber-zero): Evaluate the derived bad incidence at a flag point to split its structure-sheaf unit, then descend zero maps through residue-field extension. -> Residue-field vanishing of every local boundary test map.
- **step-successive-tensor-nilpotence** [claim](#stmt-lem-tensor-nilpotence) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-tensor-nilpotence): Use noetherian induction on coherent support, generic finite-length devissage, localization of Hom, and prime avoidance. -> A finite tensor product vanishing on the support, also with bounded coherent parameters.
- **step-simple-hecke-quotient** [claim](#stmt-boundary-simple-quotient) / [proof](#proof-stmt-boundary-simple-quotient): Base-change the ordinary Iwahori mapping module, select a cuspidal simple quotient, and exclude bad projectors through spherical-center evaluation. -> A simple quotient detecting the vector and annihilating every bad idempotent.
- **step-cuspidal-survival** [claim](#stmt-boundary-f-survives) / [proof](#proof-stmt-boundary-f-survives): Apply mapping complexes to the product fiber diagrams and quotient the all-spherical term by images with a bad factor. -> Nonvanishing of the cuspidal class under every finite product test.
- **step-final-enhancement-contradiction** [claim](#stmt-thm-main) / [proof](#proof-stmt-boundary-f-survives-2): Apply tensor vanishing on the finite-type support, transport the resulting vanishing by the mapping comparison, and contradict cuspidal survival. -> Existence of an open-orbit point and hence the full global Arthur enhancement.

          "exact_quote": "The groups $H^b_{J,V}$ have the following properties.*\n\n1.  *Their cohomology sheaves are ind-smooth on $(U^\\circ)^J$. When several legs are fused, the Weil group acts diagonally on those legs.*\n\n2.  *For every auxiliary closed point $y\\in |U^\\circ|$, the generic fiber of $H^b_{J,V}$ is finitely generated over the spherical Hecke algebra $\\mathcal H_y$. This action commutes with the partial Weil actions. Quotients by finite-codimension ideals of $\\mathcal H_y$ are finite-dimensional continuous Weil representations, defined over finite extensions of $\\mathbb Q_\\ell$ whenever the data are so defined.*\n\n3.  *The identity expressing a spherical Hecke operator as creation, partial Frobenius, and annihilation holds with arbitrary spectator legs, on the full cohomology. On cusp vectors its excursion operations agree with those defining $\\mathcal B_D$.",
          "source_id": "PM-014.1"
        }

          "anchor": "stmt-local-semisimple-slice",
          "exact_quote": "There is an affine saturated $H$-invariant open neighborhood $U_H\\subset H$ of the identity class such that the conjugation map $$\\label{local:eq-slice-map}\n L\\mathbin{\\times}^{H}(tU_H)\\longrightarrow L,\n \\qquad [g,tu]\\longmapsto gtu g^{-1},$$ is strongly étale over its image.       "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-thm-local-test-model",
          "exact_quote": "Let $t$, $H=Z_L(t)$ and $m$ be associated with a selected place as in Lemmas <a href=\"#lem:test-places\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"lem:test-places\">9</a> and <a href=\"#lem:orbit-open\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"lem:orbit-open\">10</a>. After an etale base change on invariant holonomy functions and a saturated shrinking about the identity class, the Iwahori trace has a fully faithful coherent realization. Its composite with coherent duality, the covariant Koszul functor $G_0$, and the change of degree by $m$-weight is contravariant and has the following properties.*\n\n*Its unit corresponds to $$P=\\bigoplus_bP_b\\in\\operatorname{Coh}^H(Y),\\qquad\n Y=\\{(u,e)\\in U_H\\times E_+:\\operatorname{Ad}(u)e=e\\},$$ where the $P_b$ are the derived flag direct images in <a href=\"#eq:flag-test\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:flag-test\">[eq:flag-test]</a>. The scheme $Y$ is a classical complete intersection in the smooth affine scheme $U_H\\times E_+$. The hyperspecial unit is the retract $B=\\mathcal O_Y$ of $P$ under <a href=\"#eq:spherical-line\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:spherical-line\">[eq:spherical-line]</a>. The unsheared representation comparisons are <a href=\"#eq:hom-comparison\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:hom-comparison\">[eq:hom-comparison]</a>; their internal algebra and scalar actions are <a href=\"#eq:spherical-end\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:spherical-end\">[eq:spherical-end]</a>. Before duality the slide on a label $V$ is the matrix $V(tu)$.*\n\n*The ordinary $H$-equivariant algebra $\\operatorname{End}(P)^{\\rm op}$ is the affine Hecke algebra with parameter $r$, pulled back along the etale map on its center. It has no higher cohomology, and its spherical corner is $\\operatorname{End}(B)=\\mathcal O(U_H)^H$.*\n\n*All these comparisons respect the matrix operations, homogeneous spherical maps, and neutral spectators of Proposition <a href=\"#prop:trace-model\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"prop:trace-model\">26</a>. They permit tests by every finite representation of $H/Z(L)$. In the even sectors used below, a test of $m$-weight $w$ changes cohomological degree from $b$ to $b+w$; the other sectors use the stated parity adjustment. The ring comparisons preserve the open orbit and boundary over unipotent $u$. The covariant coherent and Koszul realizations extend fully faithfully to Ind-completions; duality is applied to the compact test objects.*\n\n*The same statements hold for finite products of selected places, with external test objects, separate parity sectors, and the sum of the $m$-weights.",

Suppose $$v_i\\otimes_S^{\\mathbf L}\\kappa(\\mathfrak p)=0\n \\quad\\text{for every }i\\geq1\\text{ and every }\\mathfrak p\\in\\operatorname{Spec}S.$$ Then, for some $n\\geq1$, $$\\label{boundary:tensor-zero}\n v_1\\otimes_S^{\\mathbf L}\\cdots\\otimes_S^{\\mathbf L}v_n=0.$$ More generally, for every bounded coherent complex $K$ and every starting index $a$, there is $b\\geq a$ such that $(v_a\\otimes^{\\mathbf L}\\cdots\\otimes^{\\mathbf L}v_b)\n \\otimes^{\\mathbf L}\\operatorname{id}_K=0$.",

<a id="pm-s4"></a>
## 4. Engineering Triptych

### Panel 1 - epistemic contract

- **invariant-global-diagonal-specialization**: Under the finite-level decomposition hypothesis, a commuting algebraic Arthur factor and continuous Weil centralizer homomorphism recover the specified excursion parameter on the entire Weil group after a fixed conjugation. The centralizer need not be connected.
- **invariant-full-tuple-conjugacy**: Equality of all simultaneous invariant evaluations on finite tuples identifies homomorphisms by a single conjugator when their Zariski closures are reductive.
- **invariant-simultaneous-cohomological-bound**: The localized and sheared mapping complexes from the flag test object with finite representation coefficients have a lower bound zero independent of the finite collection of selected places and the representation.
- **invariant-restricted-ind-qc-comparison**: Passage to ordinary quasi-coherent complexes preserves mapping complexes from the thick subcategory generated by the flag test object and its finite representation twists.

### Panel 2 - negative knowledge

- **anti-use-unconditional-enhancement**: Apply the enhancement without establishing the finite-level Ramanujan–Arthur decomposition. -> The spectral input assigning the common nilpotent orbit is an explicit antecedent of the global theorem.
- **anti-use-independent-local-factors**: Treat separately chosen local compact factors as an already constructed global Weil homomorphism. -> Individual good-place semisimple classes do not supply the commuting pair or identify the specified parameter on all Weil elements.
- **anti-use-packets-and-ellipticity**: Use the commuting pair to classify Arthur packets, impose ellipticity, or calculate automorphic multiplicities. -> These conclusions are outside the stated existence and purity result.
- **anti-use-symmetric-iwahori-convolution**: Replace the Iwahori convolution structure by a symmetric monoidal structure or assume an enhanced identification of the entire central functor. -> The proved monoidal equivalence and specified neutral interchanges do not establish those stronger categorical structures.
- **anti-use-unrestricted-ind-qc-mappings**: Assume that passage from Ind-coherent to quasi-coherent objects preserves mapping complexes from arbitrary coherent sources. -> The comparison is proved for the stated thick subcategory using uniform lower bounds and the regular equation embedding.
- **anti-use-fiberwise-vanishing-as-global-zero**: Conclude that a single map is globally zero merely because it vanishes on every derived residue-field fiber. -> The argument obtains a vanishing finite tensor product from a sequence of maps; fiberwise vanishing does not itself annihilate the original map over the support ring.
- **anti-use-unverified-global-field-and-spectrum**: Extend the theorem to number fields, non-split groups, or non-cuspidal excursion spaces. -> The supplied ground data and occurring-character condition do not establish those extensions.

### Panel 3 - reference implementation and oracle

Applicability: `not_applicable`. The enhancement concerns algebraic and continuous homomorphisms recovering a parameter on the entire Weil group. Its proof uses existence of an open-orbit point and noetherian induction on support, without an executable construction or an effective bound for choosing the finite tensor product. The source states: “A noetherian tensor argument gives a finite product $q_\Pi=\boxtimes_{i=1}^nq_i$ for which $q_\Pi^*f=0$.” This does not provide a finite constructive input/output contract for certifying the global result.

<a id="pm-s6"></a>
## 6. Applicability limits and search

- **limit-spectral-input-required** (hypothesis): The global enhancement is conditional on the finite-level Ramanujan–Arthur decomposition. Blocked use: ['Invoke the global enhancement when its spectral input has not been established.']
- **limit-ground-field-and-group** (hypothesis): The ground data require a smooth projective geometrically connected finite-field curve and a split connected semisimple group. Blocked use: ['Apply the result to number fields.', 'Extend the result to nonsplit groups or arbitrary reductive groups.']
- **limit-prescribed-full-level** (formal_scope): The conclusion concerns the prescribed full finite level, including the multiplicities of its effective divisor. Arbitrary alternative level subgroups are outside the stated global theorem. Blocked use: ['Replace the full level subgroup by an arbitrary subgroup without a separate comparison theorem.', 'Require the level divisor to be reduced as an additional restriction.']
- **limit-cuspidal-occurrence** (hypothesis): The character must occur in the excursion algebra acting on the specified cuspidal function space. Blocked use: ['Apply the enhancement to an arbitrary abstract excursion character.', 'Assert the same conclusion for Eisenstein series or noncuspidal automorphic spaces.']
- **limit-coefficient-data** (hypothesis): The coefficient prime differs from the finite-field characteristic, and the algebraic coefficient embedding and square root normalization are fixed. Blocked use: ['Use coefficients of the finite-field characteristic.', 'Change the diagonal specialization normalization without tracking the chosen coefficient data.']
- **limit-local-classes-not-global-homomorphism** (formal_scope): The spectral input describes good-place semisimple classes. Its local compact factors are not required to form a homomorphism; global recovery uses the complete excursion tuple information. Blocked use: ['Assemble independently chosen local compact factors and declare that they already define the global Weil homomorphism.', 'Replace simultaneous tuple invariants by individual conjugacy classes in the reconstruction argument.']
- **limit-support-points-not-initially-continuous** (formal_scope): The global support initially records abstract homomorphisms. Continuity is supplied by the open-orbit reconstruction and recovery of the given parameter. Blocked use: ['Treat every support point as a continuous Weil parameter before reconstruction.', 'Omit the selected-place and open-orbit hypotheses when invoking the enhancement criterion.']
- **limit-no-packets-or-ellipticity** (formal_scope): Existence and purity of the commuting pair do not establish ellipticity, Arthur-packet classification, or a packet multiplicity formula. Blocked use: ['Infer ellipticity of the resulting Arthur parameter.', 'Classify Arthur packets or calculate automorphic multiplicities from this theorem.']
- **limit-full-centralizer-and-zero-orbit** (edge_case): The commuting Weil map takes values in the full algebraic centralizer, which may be disconnected. The resonance assertions include the zero orbit. Blocked use: ['Replace the target centralizer by its identity component without proof.', 'Discard the zero resonance case from the open-orbit criterion.']
- **limit-enhanced-associative-comparison** (formal_scope): The enhanced Iwahori equivalence is associative monoidal. The proved central interchanges have the stated neutral-label scope and do not identify the entire central functor in an enhanced Drinfeld center. Blocked use: ['Assume symmetric Iwahori convolution.', 'Use the restricted central comparison as an identification of the entire enhanced central functor.']
- **limit-frobenius-degree-divisibility** (hypothesis): The monoidal Frobenius intertwiner uses the selected place degree divisibility condition to remove its component tensor defect. Blocked use: ['Apply the monoidal intertwiner at an arbitrary degree without establishing the divisibility condition.']
- **limit-purity-formality-regime** (hypothesis): The formality comparison requires bounded-below complexes with degreewise finite-dimensional cohomology pure in matching degree and weight. It does not eliminate equal-weight extensions in the ambient derived category. Blocked use: ['Apply the formality statement to arbitrary mixed or unbounded-below complexes.', 'Conclude that all extensions between pure Weil modules vanish.']
- **limit-local-slice-derived-flags-and-products** (formal_scope): The coherent local tests require the specified slice base change and saturated shrinking. Their flag equations remain derived, and the product realization is asserted for the subcategory generated by external test objects. Blocked use: ['Apply the local model globally without its slice hypotheses.', 'Replace derived flag incidence by classical incidence throughout the argument.', 'Assume every coherent object on a product is an external product.']
- **limit-koszul-ind-extension** (formal_scope): The fully faithful Koszul construction uses rational equivariant coherent modules and the prescribed shear. Its Ind-extension is not an identification with an unrestricted unbounded quasi-coherent category. Blocked use: ['Replace rational weight direct sums by completed products without justification.', 'Identify the coherent Ind-category with every unbounded quasi-coherent module category.']
- **limit-nearby-comparison-and-weight-localization** (hypothesis): The nearby comparison uses neutral labels, permits omission of finitely many away places, and requires a sufficiently divisible complete Frobenius degree. The eigenvalue bound applies after the specified Hecke quotient and localization. Blocked use: ['Apply the weight bound to unreduced path cohomology.', 'Ignore the exceptional away places or use an arbitrary Frobenius power.', 'Extend the neutral-label comparison to arbitrary labels without proof.']
- **limit-constituent-occurrence-only** (formal_scope): The localized constituent inclusion controls occurrence and does not prescribe multiplicities. Blocked use: ['Compute constituent multiplicities from the inclusion theorem.', 'Replace constituent inclusion by an isomorphism of the cohomology representation with the parameter representation.']
- **limit-restricted-ind-quasicoherent-comparison** (formal_scope): The passage to ordinary quasi-coherent complexes preserves mapping complexes for sources in the specified thick subcategory generated by the flag object and finite representation twists. Blocked use: ['Assert unrestricted full faithfulness of passage from Ind-coherent to quasi-coherent objects.', 'Use an arbitrary coherent source without establishing membership in the required thick subcategory.']
- **limit-boundary-contradiction-assumption** (formal_scope): The boundary evaluation and residue-fiber vanishing statements are made under the standing assumption that the occurring character has no global enhancement. Blocked use: ['Publish universal boundary confinement as an unconditional property of global support.', 'Combine boundary vanishing with the existence theorem while suppressing the contradiction hypothesis.']
- **limit-boundary-flag-hypotheses** (hypothesis): Bad-flag detection requires unipotent residual holonomy and a vector in the boundary of the open resonance orbit; a residue-field extension may be needed. Blocked use: ['Use bad-flag detection for arbitrary residual holonomy.', 'Treat an open-orbit vector as detected by a bad flag.', 'Require the detecting flag to exist over the original residue field.']

          "exact_quote": "Let $S$ be a noetherian ring and let $v_i:D_i\\to S$, $i\\geq1$, be maps in $D(S)$ whose sources are pseudo-coherent and bounded above. Suppose $$v_i\\otimes_S^{\\mathbf L}\\kappa(\\mathfrak p)=0\n \\quad\\text{for every }i\\geq1\\text{ and every }\\mathfrak p\\in\\operatorname{Spec}S.$$ Then, for some $n\\geq1$, $$\\label{boundary:tensor-zero}\n v_1\\otimes_S^{\\mathbf L}\\cdots\\otimes_S^{\\mathbf L}v_n=0.$$ More generally, for every bounded coherent complex $K$ and every starting index $a$, there is $b\\geq a$ such that $(v_a\\otimes^{\\mathbf L}\\cdots\\otimes^{\\mathbf L}v_b)\n \\otimes^{\\mathbf L}\\operatorname{id}_K=0$.",
          "source_id": "PM-014.1"
        },
        {
          "anchor": "proof-stmt-boundary-f-survives-2",
          "exact_quote": "Under the assumption that no enhancement exists, Lemma <a href=\"#lem:tensor-nilpotence\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"lem:tensor-nilpotence\">46</a> produced a finite set of selected places for which <a href=\"#boundary:f-vanishes\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"boundary:f-vanishes\">[boundary:f-vanishes]</a> holds. The mapping comparison of Proposition <a href=\"#prop:ind-qc-comparison\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"prop:ind-qc-comparison\">42</a> gives the same vanishing in the Ind category, contradicting Proposition <a href=\"#boundary:f-survives\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"boundary:f-survives\">48</a>. Therefore the assumption was false.",
          "source_id": "PM-014.1"
        }
      ],
      "id": "limit-no-effective-stopping-or-global-algorithm",
      "kind": "nonconstructive",
      "statement": "The proof obtains a finite vanishing tensor product and forces existence of an enhancement by contradiction. An effective stopping bound, finite executable construction of the commuting pair, and computational resource bounds are ABSENT.",
      "support": "inferred"
    }
  ],
  "generation_status": "COMPLETE",
  "pm_id": "PM-014.1",
  "schema": "pm.limits.v2"
}

<a id="pm-s7"></a>
## 7. Complete mathematical paper


Under the finite-level Ramanujan–Arthur decomposition stated in Theorem 1.1, we construct a global Arthur enhancement for every occurring cuspidal excursion parameter at a specified full finite level for a split connected semisimple group over a global function field. A single algebraic SL<sub>2</sub> and a commuting Weil centralizer map recover the given parameter by diagonal specialization on the entire Weil group, including inertia. The result does not assert ellipticity, Arthur-packet classification, or a multiplicity formula.

<a id="sec:introduction"></a>
### Introduction

An Arthur parameter separates two kinds of information in an automorphic parameter: a part of weight zero and an algebraic $\operatorname{SL}_2$ that accounts for its nonzero weights. Over a global function field, the excursion construction attaches a semisimple Galois parameter to cuspidal automorphic functions. The question considered here is whether its weights come from one $\operatorname{SL}_2$ commuting with its remaining global monodromy. A decomposition of the Frobenius conjugacy class at each place does not supply such a commuting pair. The pair must recover a fixed excursion parameter on the entire Weil group.

<a id="pm-h-3"></a>
#### Excursion parameters and the statement

Let $X$ be a smooth projective geometrically connected curve over $\mathbb F_q$, let $F=\mathbb F_q(X)$, and let $G/\mathbb F_q$ be split, connected and semisimple. Fix an effective $\mathbb F_q$-defined divisor $D$, with arbitrary multiplicities, and set $U=X\setminus\operatorname{supp}(D)$. Write $\mathbb A_F$ for the adeles and $\mathcal O_{\mathbb A}$ for their integral subring. The full level subgroup is $$K_D=\ker\bigl(G(\mathcal O_{\mathbb A})\longrightarrow
                         G(\mathcal O_D)\bigr).$$ For $D=0$ it is $G(\mathcal O_{\mathbb A})$.

Fix a prime $\ell\ne\operatorname{char}(\mathbb F_q)$, put $k=\overline{\mathbb Q}_\ell$, and choose an embedding $j:\overline{\mathbb Q}\hookrightarrow k$. Let $L=\widehat G$ be the split Langlands dual group, equipped with its split rational form. Let $C_D$ be the $k$-space of compactly supported functions on $G(F)\backslash G(\mathbb A_F)/K_D$ whose constant terms along all proper $F$-parabolic subgroups vanish. Thus, if $N$ is the unipotent radical of such a subgroup, the condition is $$\int_{N(F)\backslash N(\mathbb A_F)}f(ng)\,dn=0
 \qquad(g\in G(\mathbb A_F)).$$ It is independent of the nonzero normalization of the Haar measure. The space $C_D$ is finite dimensional.

Let $\mathcal B_D$ be the commutative image of the excursion operators on $C_D$. An *occurring excursion character* is a character $\nu:\mathcal B_D\to k$ with nonzero simultaneous generalized eigenspace. Its parameter is a conjugacy class of continuous homomorphisms $$\sigma_\nu:\pi_1(U)\longrightarrow L(k)$$ defined over a finite extension of $\mathbb Q_\ell$, with reductive Zariski closure. The parameter theorem characterizes it by all excursion evaluations: for every finite set $I$, regular function $h$ on $L\backslash L^I/L$, and tuple $(\gamma_i)\in\pi_1(U)^I$, <a id="intro:excursion"></a>$$\label{intro:excursion}
 \nu(S_{I,h,(\gamma_i)})
       =h\bigl((\sigma_\nu(\gamma_i))_{i\in I}\bigr).$$ Here the quotient denotes simultaneous left and right invariance. This is the excursion parameterization, with its full tuple data .

Let $\Gamma=W_U$ be the inverse image of $\mathbb Z$ under $\pi_1(U)\to\operatorname{Gal}(\overline{\mathbb F}_q/\mathbb F_q)=\widehat\mathbb Z$, with its Weil topology. We use the Frobenius and normalized Satake conventions of ; in particular $$\deg(\operatorname{Frob}_x)=d_x=[k(x):\mathbb F_q],\qquad q_x=q^{d_x}.$$ Fix $a=j(\sqrt q)$ once and for all. A Frobenius lift and a path to the base point are chosen when an element, rather than a conjugacy class, is needed.

The finite-level Ramanujan–Arthur decomposition is our spectral input. We state precisely the consequence used below.

<a id="stmt-thm-parent"></a>
<div id="thm:parent" class="theorem">

<a id="intro:local-arthur"></a>
**Theorem 1** (Finite-level Ramanujan–Arthur decomposition ). *For every occurring excursion character $\nu$, there is a unique nilpotent $L$-orbit $\mathcal O_\nu\subset\operatorname{Lie}L$ such that, at every closed point $x\in U$, the semisimple class of $\sigma_\nu(\operatorname{Frob}_x)$ has the form $$\label{intro:local-arthur}
 \left[
  \phi_{\mathcal O_\nu}
      \begin{pmatrix}a^{d_x}&0\\0&a^{-d_x}\end{pmatrix}c_x
 \right].$$ Here $\phi_{\mathcal O_\nu}:\operatorname{SL}_2\to L$ is a Jacobson–Morozov homomorphism for $\mathcal O_\nu$ and $c_x$ is semisimple, centralizes its image, and is algebraic and conjugate into a compact subgroup at every complex embedding. The same orbit occurs at every $x$.*

</div>

The theorem is a statement about good-place semisimple classes. We use it at that scope. The choices of $c_x$ in <a href="#intro:local-arthur" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="intro:local-arthur">[intro:local-arthur]</a> are not required to arise from a homomorphism. Our result supplies the global homomorphism and retains the specified character $\nu$.

<a id="stmt-thm-main"></a>
<div id="thm:main" class="theorem">

**Theorem 2** (Global Arthur enhancement). *Assume Theorem <a href="#thm:parent" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:parent">1</a>. For every $X,G,D,\ell,j$ and every occurring excursion character $\nu$ as above, there are an algebraic homomorphism $$\phi_\nu:\operatorname{SL}_{2,k}\longrightarrow L$$ and a continuous homomorphism $$\tau_\nu:\Gamma\longrightarrow
                   Z_L\bigl(\phi_\nu(\operatorname{SL}_2)\bigr)(k),$$ both defined over a finite extension of $\mathbb Q_\ell$, with the following properties.*

1.  *The differential of $\phi_\nu$ sends $\begin{psmallmatrix}0&1\\0&0\end{psmallmatrix}$ to $\mathcal O_\nu$.*

2.  *The Zariski closure of $\tau_\nu(\Gamma)$ is reductive. For every finite-dimensional algebraic representation $r$ of the split rational form of $L$ and every closed point $x\in U$, each eigenvalue of $r(\tau_\nu(\operatorname{Frob}_x))$ lies in $j(\overline\mathbb Q)$ and its inverse image under $j$ has absolute value one under every embedding $\overline\mathbb Q\hookrightarrow\mathbb C$.*

<a id="intro:global-specialization"></a>
3.  *After one fixed $L(k)$-conjugation of $\sigma_\nu$, $$\label{intro:global-specialization}
           \sigma_\nu(w)=\tau_\nu(w)\,
               \phi_\nu\begin{pmatrix}a^{\deg(w)}&0\\0&a^{-\deg(w)}\end{pmatrix}
           \qquad(w\in\Gamma).$$*

*The centralizer is the full algebraic centralizer; it may be disconnected.*

</div>

Equivalently, $\Psi_\nu(w,h)=\tau_\nu(w)\phi_\nu(h)$ is a homomorphism $\Gamma\times\operatorname{SL}_2\to L$ with the prescribed diagonal specialization. Equation <a href="#intro:global-specialization" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="intro:global-specialization">[intro:global-specialization]</a> holds on geometric monodromy and on the inertia groups at the points of $D$, as well as on Frobenius elements. The conclusion concerns the original curve and level.

<a id="pm-h-4"></a>
#### Historical context and related work

Arthur’s conjectures explain the non-tempered part of the discrete spectrum by supplementing a tempered parameter with an algebraic $\mathrm{SL}_2$ whose image commutes with it. This structure appeared in his Maryland lectures, published in 1984, and was developed in his subsequent formulation of unipotent automorphic representations . The diagonal specialization of this additional $\mathrm{SL}_2$ accounts for the prescribed powers of the residue-field cardinality in the associated Langlands parameter. The corresponding global conjectures also concern packets and their multiplicities; the existence of a commuting pair is one part of that larger structure.

Over function fields, Drinfeld’s work for $\mathrm{GL}_2$ and Laurent Lafforgue’s work for $\mathrm{GL}_n$ realized the Langlands correspondence in the cohomology of shtukas . Laurent Lafforgue’s purity theorem for irreducible lisse sheaves with finite-order determinant supplies the weight input used here . Vincent Lafforgue subsequently constructed excursion operators and attached semisimple global parameters to cuspidal automorphic functions for reductive groups at arbitrary finite level . These operators retain invariants of tuples of Galois elements, so the resulting parameter contains more information than its individual unramified Frobenius classes. His Arthur conjecture asks that each occurring parameter arise from an elliptic global Arthur parameter.

In the everywhere unramified setting, Gaitsgory–Lafforgue–Raskin formulate the Ramanujan–Arthur decomposition by requiring the same nilpotent-orbit spectral type at every unramified place . They distinguish this condition from support on global Arthur parameters, which is formulated separately in . The finite-level theorem of , recalled in Theorem <a href="#thm:parent" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:parent">1</a>, supplies the former condition for arbitrary full level. Passing from that condition to one commuting pair requires compatibility with the complete excursion parameter, rather than a separate choice of a triple at each closed point [(intro:local-arthur)](#intro:local-arthur).

A global enhancement in the everywhere unramified setting has already been announced by Raskin as part of joint work with Gaitsgory and Lafforgue <a id="pm-cite-1"></a>[[history:RaskinAnnouncement](#ref-history:RaskinAnnouncement), Theorem C]. That announcement recovers the specified cuspidal excursion parameter from a commuting $W_X\times\mathrm{SL}_2$ parameter and asserts irreducibility of the latter, under the standing characteristic hypotheses of its Section 1.1.1. Theorem <a href="#thm:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:main">2</a> treats arbitrary full finite level under the finite-level decomposition hypothesis. Its conclusion is the existence and purity of the commuting pair stated there; the conclusion does not impose ellipticity or give a packet multiplicity formula.

The local geometry in the proof combines several established constructions. Geometric Satake identifies spherical perverse sheaves with representations of the dual group <a id="pm-cite-2"></a>[[history:MV](#ref-history:MV)]; the derived calculation of Bezrukavnikov–Finkelberg adds the polynomial directions on its Lie algebra <a id="pm-cite-3"></a>[[history:BF](#ref-history:BF)]. Gaitsgory’s nearby-cycles construction produces central sheaves in the affine flag category <a id="pm-cite-4"></a>[[history:Gaitsgory](#ref-history:Gaitsgory)]. Bezrukavnikov’s comparison of the constructible and coherent realizations of the affine Hecke category <a id="pm-cite-5"></a>[[Enh:Bezrukavnikov](#ref-Enh:Bezrukavnikov)] and the coherent trace theorem of Ben-Zvi–Chen–Helm–Nadler <a id="pm-cite-6"></a>[[Local:BZCHN](#ref-Local:BZCHN)] then provide the local coherent models. The comparisons developed below retain Frobenius, central labels, and change of level simultaneously. These compatibilities are needed to apply the local models at several places to one global support module.

The global cohomological input comes from Xue’s finiteness and smoothness theorems for stacks of shtukas <a id="pm-cite-7"></a>[[Bounds:XueFiniteness](#ref-Bounds:XueFiniteness), [Bounds:XueSmoothness](#ref-Bounds:XueSmoothness)]. They allow the excursion and partial-Frobenius formalism to be used on the cohomology carrying the local tests. The parahoric nearby-cycles comparison of Salmon <a id="pm-cite-8"></a>[[Bounds:SalmonNearby](#ref-Bounds:SalmonNearby), Theorem 5.2(1)] relates those tests to fibers at distinct points. The more general restricted-coefficient comparison is developed by Eteve–Xue <a id="pm-cite-9"></a>[[Bounds:EteveXueNearby](#ref-Bounds:EteveXueNearby)]. The parahoric comparison, together with the constituent purity theorem, brings the cohomological weight bound into the simultaneous local calculation.

<a id="pm-h-5"></a>
#### The proof mechanism

Put $A=L_{\mathrm{ad}}$ and $\mathfrak g=\operatorname{Lie}L=\operatorname{Lie}A$. We first attach to a nonzero $\nu$-eigenvector $f\in C_D$ a finite-type affine scheme $\operatorname{Spec}R$. A point of this scheme records a homomorphism $b:\Gamma\to A(k)$ and an element $e\in\mathfrak g$ satisfying <a id="intro:scaling"></a>$$\label{intro:scaling}
       \operatorname{Ad}(b(w))e=q^{\deg(w)}e\qquad(w\in\Gamma).$$ The invariant evaluations of every tuple of $b$ are those of $\sigma_{\nu,\mathrm{ad}}$. The construction uses matrix entries of partial Weil actions on regular Satake labels, together with a degree-two operation. It is this tuple information that eventually recovers the chosen global parameter.

At suitably chosen good places $x$, choose a representative $t_x$ of the semisimple Frobenius class and consider the resonance space $$E_{t_x}=\{e\in\mathfrak g:\operatorname{Ad}(t_x)e=q_xe\}.$$ The connected centralizer of $t_x$ has a unique open orbit, whose elements belong to $\mathcal O_\nu$. If a point of $\operatorname{Spec}R$ reaches this open orbit at even one test place, the scaling relation yields a global commuting pair. Levi projection and the full tuple identities give the required simultaneous conjugation; a norm argument then proves weight zero. Consequently, failure of Theorem <a href="#thm:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:main">2</a> would force the image of $\operatorname{Spec}R$ into the boundary at every test place.

The rest of the proof rules out this boundary condition. At the $i$th test place, a coherent comparison identifies the hyperspecial object with the structure sheaf $B_i=\mathcal O_{Y_i}$ of an ordinary complete intersection $Y_i$. Let $P_i^{\mathrm{bad}}$ be the sum of the derived direct images of structure sheaves from those local flag spaces whose vector images miss the open orbit. Their structure-sheaf units define $$Q_i=\operatorname{fib}(B_i\longrightarrow P_i^{\mathrm{bad}}),\qquad
 q_i:Q_i\longrightarrow B_i.$$ Every boundary point supplied by the global support lies under one of these flag spaces. On its derived residue-field fiber, evaluation at a flag splits the unit map, so the fiber map $q_i$ is zero.

Shtuka smoothness, nearby cycles and constituent purity give one lower cohomological bound, independent of the number of test places. This permits the local tests to act on the global support in ordinary quasi-coherent complexes. If every local image of $\operatorname{Spec}R$ lies in the boundary, each $q_i$ therefore vanishes on every derived residue-field fiber of this support. A noetherian tensor argument gives a finite product $q_\Pi=\boxtimes_{i=1}^nq_i$ for which $q_\Pi^*f=0$.

On the other hand, a simple quotient of the cuspidal Iwahori Hecke module detects $f$ and is annihilated by the idempotents of all the summands in $P_i^{\mathrm{bad}}$. Applying shtuka cohomology to the defining fiber diagrams for $Q_i$ shows that $f$ survives their product: $q_\Pi^*f\ne0$. This contradiction forces an open-orbit point and completes the global enhancement. Figure <a href="#fig:proof-architecture" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="fig:proof-architecture">1</a> records the dependencies of these two conclusions.

<figure id="fig:proof-architecture">
<embed src="figures/proof-architecture.pdf" />
<figcaption>The two uses of the global cyclic support. Full excursion tuple data identify the parameter when an open-orbit point exists. If every test sees boundary support, simultaneous coherent tests give incompatible vanishing and nonvanishing statements for the same cusp vector.</figcaption>
</figure>

<a id="pm-h-6"></a>
#### Technical contributions and organization

Three comparisons carry most of the additional work. The first retains matrix entries and the degree-two operation simultaneously in a finite-type global support. The second constructs the enhanced Frobenius-compatible Iwahori realization and compares the particular holonomy and higher morphisms used by the spherical trace. These are stronger requirements than a comparison of Hecke algebras or characters. The third obtains a simultaneous bound through a one-leg nearby-cycles theorem, using Weil restriction to place the chosen degenerations in one fiber. The argument also isolates a tensor-vanishing lemma for a sequence of maps from pseudo-coherent bounded-above complexes; its proof is independent of the automorphic setting.

We also use the local constructions and constituent purity proved in <a id="pm-cite-10"></a>[[Parent](#ref-Parent), Sections 2–3 and Lemma 6.4], with their stated compatibilities. In particular, spherical Satake and path rotation are enhanced <a id="pm-cite-11"></a>[[Parent](#ref-Parent), Theorem 2.2 and Proposition 2.5]. The central-sheaf functor takes values in the Drinfeld center of the homotopy category. For each fixed central label its interchange is a natural equivalence of enhanced exact functors in the other kernel variable; the central tensor identities hold in the homotopy category . The resulting Hecke algebra action is on cohomology <a id="pm-cite-12"></a>[[Parent](#ref-Parent), Proposition 2.6]. The additional enhanced Iwahori comparison and its compatibility with the trace transfers are proved in Sections <a href="#sec:enhancement" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:enhancement">4</a>–<a href="#sec:local-models" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:local-models">6</a>.

Section <a href="#sec:global-support" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:global-support">2</a> constructs the cyclic support, and Section <a href="#sec:open-orbit" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:open-orbit">3</a> proves the open-orbit criterion for the global enhancement. Section <a href="#sec:enhancement" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:enhancement">4</a> constructs the enhanced local realization. Sections <a href="#sec:trace" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:trace">5</a> and <a href="#sec:local-models" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:local-models">6</a> establish the transfer maps and coherent tests. Section <a href="#sec:lower-bound" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:lower-bound">7</a> proves their simultaneous cohomological bound. Section <a href="#sec:boundary" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:boundary">8</a> combines the tests and proves Theorem <a href="#thm:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:main">2</a>.

<a id="pm-h-7"></a>
#### Conventions

All categories used in traces or tensor products have their stable dg enhancements; all coends are derived. The notation $\operatorname{Coh}$ means bounded derived coherent complexes, and shifts are cohomological. A *neutral* representation of $L$ is one factoring through $A$. On these representations the component parity correction in geometric Satake is trivial. Satake kernels are normalized relative to the positions of their legs, with the shifts and Tate twists of <a id="pm-cite-13"></a>[[Parent](#ref-Parent)]; thus $k(-1)$ has Frobenius $q$. The comparison of actual trace operations always retains these normalizations. When a single complex absolute value is used in a weight estimate, we fix a complex realization extending the chosen embedding of algebraic coefficients.

<a id="sec:global-support"></a>
### A global support from shtuka cohomology

Fix an occurring excursion character $\nu$ and its parameter $\sigma_\nu$. The first step is to retain the matrix entries of this parameter in a commutative algebra acting on shtuka cohomology. Derived Satake supplies an additional Lie-algebra coordinate $e$. The resulting algebra will parametrize homomorphisms $b$ with the full excursion invariants and the equation $\operatorname{Ad}(b(w))e=q^{\deg(w)}e$. Its finite type is important: later we will test one and the same support at arbitrarily many closed points.

Write $L=\widehat G$, $A=L_{\mathrm{ad}}$, and $\mathfrak g=\operatorname{Lie}L=\operatorname{Lie}A$. A representation of $L$ is called *neutral* if it factors through $A$. On these representations the component-parity correction in geometric Satake is trivial. We use the shifts relative to the positions of the legs and the Frobenius convention of <a id="pm-cite-14"></a>[[Parent](#ref-Parent), Section 2]; in particular, Frobenius on $k(-1)$ is $q$. All representation categories in this section have coefficients in $k=\overline{\mathbb Q}_\ell$.

<a id="pm-h-9"></a>
#### The cohomology and its Weil actions

We first allow either the prescribed full level $D$, or that level together with Iwahori level at a finite set $M\subset |U|$. Let $U^\circ$ be the corresponding good open, namely $U$ or $U\setminus M$, and put $\Gamma=W_{U^\circ}$. For a finite set $J$ and $V\in\operatorname{Rep}(L^J)$, denote by $H^b_{J,V}$ the degree-$b$ compact cohomology of the stack of shtukas with Satake coefficient $V$, in the filtered limit over Harder–Narasimhan bounds. We use the same notation for its fibers, specifying paths when they matter. The normalization is relative to $(U^\circ)^J$, so fusion introduces no change in $b$.

<a id="stmt-lem-glob-cohomology"></a>
<div id="lem:glob-cohomology" class="lemma">

**Lemma 3** (Cohomological inputs). *The groups $H^b_{J,V}$ have the following properties.*

1.  *Their cohomology sheaves are ind-smooth on $(U^\circ)^J$. Geometric transport and partial Frobenius give an action of $\Gamma^J$ on the transported fibers, compatible with fusion. When several legs are fused, the Weil group acts diagonally on those legs.*

2.  *For every auxiliary closed point $y\in |U^\circ|$, the generic fiber of $H^b_{J,V}$ is finitely generated over the spherical Hecke algebra $\mathcal H_y$. This action commutes with the partial Weil actions. Quotients by finite-codimension ideals of $\mathcal H_y$ are finite-dimensional continuous Weil representations, defined over finite extensions of $\mathbb Q_\ell$ whenever the data are so defined.*

3.  *The identity expressing a spherical Hecke operator as creation, partial Frobenius, and annihilation holds with arbitrary spectator legs, on the full cohomology. On cusp vectors its excursion operations agree with those defining $\mathcal B_D$.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-lem-glob-cohomology"><summary>Proof of stmt-lem-glob-cohomology</summary>

*Proof.* We use the fusion, partial Frobenius, and Hecke constructions of V. Lafforgue <a id="pm-cite-15"></a>[[Hist:VLafforgue](#ref-Hist:VLafforgue), Propositions 4.12, 4.14, and 6.2], the finiteness and excursion theorems of Xue <a id="pm-cite-16"></a>[[Bounds:XueFiniteness](#ref-Bounds:XueFiniteness), Theorem 2 and Sections 3.2–3.7], and Xue’s ind-smoothness theorem <a id="pm-cite-17"></a>[[Bounds:XueSmoothness](#ref-Bounds:XueSmoothness), Theorem 4.2.3 and Proposition 5.0.4]. These results permit an arbitrary finite level subscheme. The central lattice quotient in their statements may be taken trivial because $G$ is semisimple. Additional Iwahori level is obtained from full level at the added points by invariants under a finite level group. In characteristic zero this is a direct summand, so the same assertions hold there, with the added points removed from $U^\circ$.

For clarity, the product Weil action does not require a product formula for the geometric fundamental group of an open curve. The path group with partial Frobenius has quotient $\mathbb Z^J$ and geometric kernel the ordinary geometric fundamental group of $(U^\circ)^J$. It maps onto $\Gamma^J$; on degree-zero kernels, surjectivity follows by fixing all but one coordinate. Drinfeld’s lemma for finite-dimensional continuous representations factors its action through $\Gamma^J$ <a id="pm-cite-18"></a>[[Bounds:XueFiniteness](#ref-Bounds:XueFiniteness), Lemmas 3.2.10 and 3.3.2]. One may see the finite-dimensional assertion from its finite-cover version as follows. The compact geometric image is a compact $\ell$-adic analytic group and has open characteristic subgroups forming a neighborhood basis. Attach the degrees to the image and quotient by one of these subgroups. The result is finite-by-$\mathbb Z^J$ and is residually finite: after a finite-index passage the finite kernel is central, and sufficiently divisible powers of the remaining generators give an abelian subgroup of finite index. The finite-cover lemma therefore kills the kernel of the map to $\Gamma^J$ in every such quotient, hence in the original representation.

Apply this to the finite-dimensional quotients in (ii). Their geometric actions are continuous by ind-smoothness, or by taking images of finite stages at a geometric generic point. Finite generation over the noetherian algebra $\mathcal H_y$ implies $$\bigcap_{\mathfrak m}\bigcap_{n\geq 1}
       \mathfrak m^n H^b_{J,V}=0,$$ where $\mathfrak m$ ranges over maximal ideals. Thus these quotients detect the action on the whole module, as in <a id="pm-cite-19"></a>[[Bounds:XueFiniteness](#ref-Bounds:XueFiniteness), Lemma 3.2.13]. Hecke operators commute with the partial actions in disjoint position; smoothness then gives the transported assertions. Finally, fusion identifies the action on a fused leg with the product of its partial Frobenius maps. The creation and annihilation morphisms are the same ones on full and Hecke-finite cohomology, which proves (iii). ◻

</details>

We will also need the usual compact-cohomology weight bound, in the normalization just fixed. Here and below a weight is measured using one fixed complex realization of the coefficient field.

<a id="stmt-lem-glob-weights"></a>
<div id="lem:glob-weights" class="lemma">

**Lemma 4** (Complete Frobenius weights). *Let the legs be at pairwise distinct points of $U^\circ$ rational over $\mathbb F_{q^d}$, with external Satake labels. Every eigenvalue of complete $q^d$-Frobenius on a finite-dimensional subquotient of $H^b_{J,V}$ has weight at most $b$.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-lem-glob-weights"><summary>Proof of stmt-lem-glob-weights</summary>

*Proof.* With the path normalization of <a id="pm-cite-20"></a>[[Parent](#ref-Parent), Section 2], the coefficient pulled back from Satake is a mixed complex of weights at most zero: its degree-$a$ stalk cohomology has weights at most $a$. On each finite-type Harder–Narasimhan open, compact direct image consequently has weights at most $b$ in degree $b$. The bounded-leg shtuka stacks have Deligne–Mumford opens with finite stabilizers; the same bound follows either directly for these stacks or by stratifying and using coarse spaces with exact finite-group invariants. The complete Frobenius obtained from the partial actions is the cohomological Frobenius, by the cyclicity of the path functor <a id="pm-cite-21"></a>[[Parent](#ref-Parent), Section 2, Proposition ``Coherent path rotation'']. The bound passes to the filtered limit and its finite-dimensional subquotients. ◻

</details>

<a id="pm-h-10"></a>
#### Matrix entries and a Lie-algebra coordinate

<a id="eq:regular-tests"></a>
Choose a geometric base point in $U^\circ$ and use it in every factor. For representatives $V_\lambda$ of the simple objects of $\operatorname{Rep}(A)$, form the rational $A$-module $$M_b^{\mathrm{reg}}
   =\bigoplus_\lambda H^b_{\{1\},V_\lambda}\otimes V_\lambda^*.$$ The action of $A$ is on the coefficient factors $V_\lambda^*$. Semisimplicity and fusion give, naturally in the neutral labels $V_i$, $$\label{eq:regular-tests}
 \left(M_b^{\mathrm{reg}}\otimes\bigotimes_{i\in J}V_i\right)^A
       \simeq H^b_{J,\boxtimes_{i\in J}V_i}.$$ We now keep the individual matrix entries of the Weil actions on the right-hand side.

For $V\in\operatorname{Rep}(A)$ and $w\in\Gamma$, define the $A$-equivariant map $$T_V(w):M_b^{\mathrm{reg}}\otimes V
                 \longrightarrow M_b^{\mathrm{reg}}\otimes V$$ by the following tests. After tensoring with any $W\in\operatorname{Rep}(A)$ and taking invariants, use <a href="#eq:regular-tests" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:regular-tests">[eq:regular-tests]</a> for $V\boxtimes W$ and act by $w$ on the $V$-leg. These maps are natural in $W$. Since rational $A$-modules are semisimple, such tests determine a unique equivariant map, also when their multiplicity spaces are infinite.

Let $\mathscr B_\Gamma$ be the coordinate algebra of the scheme $\operatorname{Hom}(\Gamma,A)$, with $\Gamma$ regarded as an abstract group. Equivalently, for a commutative $k$-algebra $B$, its $B$-points are the homomorphisms $\Gamma\to A(B)$. This algebra need not be of finite type. Denote its universal homomorphism by $\mathbf b$.

<a id="stmt-lem-glob-matrices"></a>
<div id="lem:glob-matrices" class="lemma">

**Lemma 5** (The matrix-entry action). *The matrices $T_V(w)$ define an $A$-equivariant action of $\mathscr B_\Gamma$ on each $M_b^{\mathrm{reg}}$, with $A$ acting on $\operatorname{Hom}(\Gamma,A)$ by conjugation. For a good closed point $y$, the function $\operatorname{Tr}_V(\mathbf b(\operatorname{Frob}_y))$ acts by the spherical Hecke operator with label $V$.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-lem-glob-matrices"><summary>Proof of stmt-lem-glob-matrices</summary>

*Proof.* The entries of $T_V(w)$ and $T_W(w')$ commute: test both maps using separate $V$- and $W$-legs and use the product Weil action of Lemma <a href="#lem:glob-cohomology" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:glob-cohomology">3</a>. Fusion and the group law give $$T_{V\otimes W}(w)=T_V(w)T_W(w),\qquad
 T_V(w)T_V(w')=T_V(ww'),\qquad T_{\mathbf1}(w)=1.$$ In the first identity the two matrices act on their respective tensor factors. Together with naturality in representations and duality, these are the tensor, invertibility, and group-law relations presenting $\mathscr B_\Gamma$. The construction is equivariant by definition of the tests. Contracting the $V$- and $V^*$-labels identifies its invariant trace with creation, partial Frobenius, and annihilation. The Hecke identity with spectators proves the last assertion. ◻

</details>

<a id="eq:degree-two"></a>
The graded module $M_\bullet^{\mathrm{reg}}$ carries a further commuting operation. Choose a nonempty open $U_0\subset U^\circ$ and an *étale* coordinate $t:U_0\to\mathbb A^1$ over $\mathbb F_q$. Derived Satake identifies its local spherical category with $\operatorname{Perf}^L(\operatorname{Sym}(\mathfrak g^*[-2]))$ and all morphisms monoidally <a id="pm-cite-22"></a>[[Parent](#ref-Parent), Section 2, Theorem ``Derived Satake with Frobenius'']. In particular, the identity tensor in $\mathfrak g^*\otimes\mathfrak g$ defines $$\label{eq:degree-two}
 \epsilon:\mathbf1\longrightarrow\operatorname{Sat}(\mathfrak g)[2].$$ Frobenius multiplies this morphism by $q$.

<a id="stmt-lem-glob-degree-two"></a>
<div id="lem:glob-degree-two" class="lemma">

<a id="eq:global-relation"></a>
**Lemma 6** (The degree-two operation). *The morphism <a href="#eq:degree-two" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:degree-two">[eq:degree-two]</a> induces an equivariant map $$E_b:M_b^{\mathrm{reg}}\longrightarrow
                 M_{b+2}^{\mathrm{reg}}\otimes\mathfrak g.$$ Its coordinates commute with each other and with the $\mathscr B_\Gamma$-action. Writing $\mathbf e$ for the resulting Lie-algebra coordinate, so that its linear functions have degree two, one has the identity of operators $$\label{eq:global-relation}
           \operatorname{Ad}(\mathbf b(w))\mathbf e
                         =q^{\deg(w)}\mathbf e
          \qquad(w\in\Gamma).$$ These operations commute with all good-place Hecke actions.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-lem-glob-degree-two"><summary>Proof of stmt-lem-glob-degree-two</summary>

*Proof.* First globalize the local morphism over the chosen coordinate open. For a moving leg $v$, the parameter $z=t(x)-t(v)$ identifies its formal disc with the standard formal disc. The local equivariant kernel and $\epsilon$ therefore define a relative kernel morphism over $U_0$. With other legs present, apply this morphism in one step of the iterated Hecke stack. On bounded modifications the twisted products and their morphisms descend along the trivialization torsors, using a sufficiently large finite jet quotient. Pullback to the Frobenius path stack and compact direct image in the bounds limit give maps of cohomology sheaves over powers of the geometric open. The direct image merging successive modifications is proper, so proper base change identifies the restriction to coincident legs with convolution by $\epsilon$.

<a id="glob:eq-relative-epsilon"></a>
More explicitly, write $\mathcal H^b_{J,V}$ for the ind-smooth cohomology sheaf whose fibers are $H^b_{J,V}$. Fix a distinguished leg $i\in J$ and an external neutral label $W$ on the remaining legs. The construction just given, applied to the Frobenius-invariant Tate twist $\mathbf1\to\operatorname{Sat}(\mathfrak g)[2](1)$ of $\epsilon$, is a map of the relative coefficient complexes on the iterated shtuka stack. Compact direct image gives the underlying geometric map $$\label{glob:eq-relative-epsilon}
 \mathcal H^b_{J,\mathbf1_i\boxtimes W}|_{U_0^J}
       \longrightarrow
 \mathcal H^{b+2}_{J,\mathfrak g_i\boxtimes W}|_{U_0^J}(1).$$ Thus this map exists on the entire coordinate product, including the diagonals. It is not defined by extension from disjoint legs.

Using this map with a spectator label and then <a href="#eq:regular-tests" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:regular-tests">[eq:regular-tests]</a> gives $E_b$. Its naturality in spectator representations is the convolution naturality on the diagonal. The tensor and composition rules in derived Satake show that the coordinates of $E$ commute. More explicitly, the two ways of applying $\epsilon$ are both the tensor square of the identity tensor in $\mathfrak g^*\otimes\mathfrak g$; interchanging the two neutral adjoint labels is the ordinary flip. The enhanced monoidal identification includes these morphisms, and degree two introduces no Koszul sign.

We now check the partial Weil structures on <a href="#glob:eq-relative-epsilon" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="glob:eq-relative-epsilon">[glob:eq-relative-epsilon]</a>. On the target, denote by $(1_i)$ the Tate twist equipped with partial Frobenius multiplied by $q^{-1}$ in the $i$th variable and unchanged in the other variables. Its total Frobenius is the ordinary Tate-twisted Frobenius. The map is compatible with these structures. After forgetting the twist, this says that it commutes with partial Frobenius on every spectator leg, whereas on its own leg $$F_{\mathrm{target}}\epsilon
                   =q\,\epsilon F_{\mathrm{source}}.$$ To check these equalities of cohomology-sheaf maps, work first where all modifications, including their Frobenius moves, are disjoint. A spectator partial Frobenius changes only its own factor of the twisted product and commutes with the morphism on the distinguished factor. The distinguished partial Frobenius instead pulls that morphism through Frobenius and its Weil structure. Since the coordinate is defined over $\mathbb F_q$, the latter comparison is exactly the local scaling by $q$ for $\epsilon$. Evaluation on a geometric generic fiber is faithful for maps of ind-smooth sheaves, as is seen on their finite-dimensional smooth stages. Both equalities therefore hold on all of $U_0^J$. Restriction to a fusion diagonal uses the proper-base-change identification already established, so the same equalities hold for the fused tests. Because <a href="#glob:eq-relative-epsilon" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="glob:eq-relative-epsilon">[glob:eq-relative-epsilon]</a> is a geometric sheaf map, it also commutes with geometric transport. Lemma <a href="#lem:glob-cohomology" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:glob-cohomology">3</a> then gives the asserted compatibility with each partial Weil action.

Spectator compatibility, tested against the matrix entries of $T_V(w)$, proves that those entries commute with the coordinates of $E$. On the distinguished adjoint leg the source label is the unit; hence the second compatibility gives $T_\mathfrak g(w)E_b=q^{\deg(w)}E_b$. Since $T_\mathfrak g(w)=\operatorname{Ad}(\mathbf b(w))$, this is <a href="#eq:global-relation" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:global-relation">[eq:global-relation]</a>. It suffices to prove these assertions on $U_0$: the map $W_{U_0}\to\Gamma$ is surjective. Finally, a good-place Hecke correspondence can be taken disjoint from the moving legs at a geometric generic point. It then commutes with the construction, and transport gives the stated Hecke compatibility. ◻

</details>

We have thus constructed a graded action of the commutative algebra $$\mathscr R_\Gamma=
 \frac{\mathscr B_\Gamma\otimes\operatorname{Sym}(\mathfrak g^*)}
 {\bigl(\text{coefficients of }
       \operatorname{Ad}(\mathbf b(w))\mathbf e-q^{\deg(w)}\mathbf e,
                                      \ w\in\Gamma\bigr)}.$$ The matrix coordinates have degree zero and the linear $\mathbf e$ coordinates have degree two. We retain this grading on cyclic quotients, but use the ordinary underlying affine scheme when speaking of their points or support.

<a id="pm-h-11"></a>
#### Comparison with fixed-place path traces

The preceding action must agree with the local coordinates used in the later tests. We establish the comparison here while the cohomological construction is explicit.

Let $x_1,\ldots,x_n$ be distinct closed points of $U_0$, put $d_i=\deg(x_i)$ and $r_i=q^{d_i}$, and choose paths defining $\gamma_i=\operatorname{Frob}_{x_i}\in\Gamma$. In each geometric Frobenius cycle, place $\operatorname{Sat}(V_i)$ at one selected point and the unit at the other points. The spectral description of the hyperspecial path trace <a id="pm-cite-23"></a>[[Parent](#ref-Parent), Section 2, Proposition ``Spectral form of a Frobenius trace''] uses the dg algebra $$B_r=\mathcal O(L)\otimes\operatorname{Sym}(\mathfrak g^*[-2]\oplus\mathfrak g^*[-1]),\qquad
 d\eta_\alpha=\langle\alpha,\operatorname{Ad}(s)e-re\rangle.$$ Here $s\in L$ is the holonomy coordinate; $e$ has linear functions in degree two, and $\eta_\alpha$ has degree one. Write $\mathcal M_n$ for the cohomology of the corresponding equivariant spectral module, restricted to the sector on which $Z(L)^n$ acts trivially.

<a id="stmt-lem-glob-frames"></a>
<div id="lem:glob-frames" class="lemma">

<a id="eq:framed-module"></a>
<a id="eq:framed-action"></a>
**Lemma 7** (Frames and local coordinates). *As graded rational $A^n$-modules, $$\label{eq:framed-module}
 \mathcal M_n\simeq\operatorname{Ind}_A^{A^n}M_\bullet^{\mathrm{reg}}
        =\bigl(\mathcal O(A^n)\otimes M_\bullet^{\mathrm{reg}}\bigr)^A.$$ If $g_i\in A$ are the frame coordinates, with diagonal change of frame $g_i\mapsto g_i h^{-1}$, then the adjoint holonomy and degree-two coordinates act as $$\label{eq:framed-action}
 s_{i,\mathrm{ad}}=g_i\mathbf b(\gamma_i)g_i^{-1},
       \qquad e_i=g_i\mathbf e g_i^{-1}.$$*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-lem-glob-frames"><summary>Proof of stmt-lem-glob-frames</summary>

*Proof.* Testing the left side by $\boxtimes_i V_i$ gives the compact path cohomology of the selected labels. Transport and <a href="#eq:regular-tests" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:regular-tests">[eq:regular-tests]</a> identify this with $(M_\bullet^{\mathrm{reg}}\otimes\bigotimes_i V_i)^A$. Frobenius reciprocity identifies the same test on the right side of <a href="#eq:framed-module" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:framed-module">[eq:framed-module]</a>; semisimplicity proves that equation. The description of $e_i$ follows from the construction of $\epsilon$ on the selected leg.

For holonomy one must check the entire matrix, not only its trace. The local full-cycle slide acts on a representation label by $V_i(s_i)$, by the cited spectral trace proposition. Keep a spectator label at the same place, and before fusion retain their ordered successive modifications, with the sliding leg at the rotating end. Moving that leg through Frobenius to the other end is precisely the partial Frobenius morphism in the iterated-modification definition of shtukas. The Weil structure identifies the induced slide on cohomology with the positive-degree partial Frobenius action. The passage between orders uses the same proper convolution direct images and Satake fusion maps as coalescence. If $d_i>1$, the intermediate moves are disjoint from the spectator, and the returning move uses this Satake interchange. The full cycle consequently acts by $T_{V_i}(\gamma_i)$ with every spectator, proving the matrix identity in <a href="#eq:framed-action" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:framed-action">[eq:framed-action]</a>. All comparisons use the product Weil actions of Lemma <a href="#lem:glob-cohomology" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:glob-cohomology">3</a>, so are compatible as the finite set of legs varies. ◻

</details>

<a id="pm-h-12"></a>
#### The finite-type cyclic support

Return to level $D$ and hence $\Gamma=W_U$. The nonzero generalized $\nu$-eigenspace contains a simultaneous eigenvector $0\ne f\in C_D$: a commuting finite-dimensional algebra over an algebraically closed field has a nonzero socle in every nonzero local module. Empty legs identify $C_D$ with the cuspidal subspace of $(M_0^{\mathrm{reg}})^A$. Define $$R=\mathscr R_\Gamma/\operatorname{Ann}(f).$$

<a id="stmt-prop-global-support"></a>
<div id="prop:global-support" class="proposition">

<a id="eq:cyclic-support"></a>
**Proposition 8** (Global support). *The algebra $R$ is a nonzero finitely generated graded $k$-algebra with rational $A$-action. It has a universal homomorphism $\mathbf b:\Gamma\to A(R)$ and an equivariant element $\mathbf e\in\mathfrak g\otimes R$ satisfying <a href="#eq:global-relation" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:global-relation">[eq:global-relation]</a>. There is an equivariant graded injection $$\label{eq:cyclic-support}
                   R\hookrightarrow M_\bullet^{\mathrm{reg}},
                          \qquad 1\longmapsto f.$$ For every finite tuple $(w_i)_{i\in I}$ and every $F\in\mathcal O(A^I)^A$, its value on the universal tuple is the scalar $$F\bigl((\mathbf b(w_i))_{i\in I}\bigr)
       =F\bigl((\sigma_{\nu,\mathrm{ad}}(w_i))_{i\in I}\bigr)
                                                   \quad\text{in }R.$$ In particular the full simultaneous-conjugacy data of the excursion parameter are retained by $\operatorname{Spec}R$.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-prop-global-support"><summary>Proof of stmt-prop-global-support</summary>

*Proof.* All assertions except finite type and the invariant evaluations follow from the construction. The annihilator is graded because $f$ is homogeneous and is $A$-stable because $f$ is invariant. For the invariant evaluation, append an identity variable to convert $F$ into a function invariant under simultaneous left and right multiplication. The action of this function is the corresponding excursion operation, by creation and annihilation in <a href="#eq:regular-tests" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:regular-tests">[eq:regular-tests]</a>. Lemma <a href="#lem:glob-cohomology" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:glob-cohomology">3</a>(iii) identifies its action on $f$ with $\nu$. Commutativity then gives the asserted identity throughout the cyclic module $R$.

Choose one auxiliary good point $y_0$. All operations producing <a href="#eq:cyclic-support" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:cyclic-support">[eq:cyclic-support]</a> commute with $\mathcal H_{y_0}$, so their values on $f$ belong to its simultaneous Hecke eigenspace. Choose finitely many representations whose matrix coefficients generate $\mathcal O(A)$. As $w$ varies in $\Gamma$, the corresponding matrix entries applied to $f$ have degree zero and lie in a fixed finite list of $A$-isotypic components. In each such component the multiplicity space is finitely generated over the noetherian algebra $\mathcal H_{y_0}$. Its submodule annihilated by the Hecke maximal ideal is therefore finite-dimensional over $k$. Hence the entries under consideration span a finite-dimensional subspace of $R$. Finitely many of them generate all degree-zero matrix coordinates as an algebra. Adding the finitely many linear coordinates of $\mathbf e$ proves that $R$ is of finite type. ◻

</details>

We will use $y_0$ as the fixed auxiliary Hecke place. The construction up to this final cyclic quotient remains available at every auxiliary Iwahori level, a fact needed for the weight argument in Section <a href="#sec:lower-bound" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:lower-bound">7</a>.

<a id="sec:open-orbit"></a>
### An open-orbit criterion for enhancement

The algebra $R$ of Proposition <a href="#prop:global-support" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:global-support">8</a> records abstract homomorphisms with the full excursion invariants of $\sigma_{\nu,\mathrm{ad}}$. Its points need not initially be continuous. We prove that a point whose Lie coordinate lies in one specified open orbit already yields the continuous enhancement of Theorem <a href="#thm:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:main">2</a>. The remaining sections will force such a point to exist.

<a id="pm-h-14"></a>
#### Places with connected centralizers

For $x\in |U_0|$, let $t_x\in L$ be a torus representative of the spherical class acting on $f$, in the holonomy convention of <a id="pm-cite-24"></a>[[Parent](#ref-Parent), Section 2]. Its image $t_{x,\mathrm{ad}}\in A$ represents the semisimple class of $\sigma_{\nu,\mathrm{ad}}(\operatorname{Frob}_x)$. The full-group convention may differ from another normalized Satake presentation by a finite central sign. This does not change the adjoint class, any absolute value exponent, or any centralizer. Write $L/\!/L$ and $A/\!/A$ for the affine conjugation quotients. Let $\Omega$ be the finite group of length-zero elements of the extended affine Weyl group of $G$.

<a id="stmt-lem-test-places"></a>
<div id="lem:test-places" class="lemma">

**Lemma 9** (Choice of test places). *There are infinitely many distinct points $x_i\in |U_0|\setminus\{y_0\}$, with $d_i=\deg(x_i)$ divisible by $|\Omega|$, such that, for $t=t_{x_i}$,*

1.  *$H=Z_L(t)$ and $Z_A(t_{\mathrm{ad}})$ are connected;*

2.  *near the class of $t_{\mathrm{ad}}$, the map $L/\!/L\to A/\!/A$ is a $Z(L)$-torsor, and the central torsor $L\to A$ is its pullback. In particular, $Z_A(t_{\mathrm{ad}})=H/Z(L)$.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-lem-test-places"><summary>Proof of stmt-lem-test-places</summary>

*Proof.* Choose a faithful representation of $A$. The parameter has compact image in its matrices over a finite extension of $\mathbb Q_\ell$. Chebotarev applied to a sufficiently small finite quotient of this image gives infinitely many Frobenius classes in any prescribed identity congruence neighborhood. Include the constant-field quotient modulo $|\Omega|$ to impose the degree condition. We may discard $y_0$ and the finitely many points outside $U_0$.

All eigenvalues of these matrices are as close to one as required.
