Via <a href="#boundary:hecke-base-change" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="boundary:hecke-base-change">[boundary:hecke-base-change]</a>, extend its action to the entire algebra $\mathcal H$ by evaluating $\mathcal Z_{\mathrm{sl}}$ at the selected identity classes. The image algebra is unchanged by this scalar extension, so the resulting module is still simple; in particular the new flag idempotents on the slice act on it. Its central values are precisely those used in the localization at $\mathfrak m$, so it remains a quotient after localization. This construction transfers back to $k$ through the chosen complex realization and gives the required simple module, denoted by $S$.

Let $e_{\mathrm{sph}}$ be the idempotent of the spherical summand $B$, and let $e_b$ project to a bad summand in one factor of $P$. If both idempotents act nontrivially on $S$, simplicity implies that $e_{\mathrm{sph}}\mathcal H e_b\mathcal H e_{\mathrm{sph}}$ acts nontrivially on $e_{\mathrm{sph}}S$. For example, generate $S$ from $e_{\mathrm{sph}}S$, project to $e_bS$, and then generate back to $e_{\mathrm{sph}}S$. Some composite from $B$ through that bad summand and back to $B$ must therefore act nontrivially.

<a id="boundary:spherical-invariants"></a>
Such a composite belongs to $$\label{boundary:spherical-invariants}
 \operatorname{End}_{H^\natural}(B)
      =\bigotimes_{i=1}^n k[U_{H_i}]^{H_i},$$ with the corresponding equality after any localization of these local center rings. To justify the equality, $\operatorname{End}(B)$ before invariants is $\mathcal O(Y)$, and $m_i$ acts trivially on $U_{H_i}$ while all linear functions in $e_i$ have strictly negative $m_i$-weight. Thus an invariant function is independent of the $e_i$. This observation also identifies its action on $S$ with evaluation at the selected quotient classes.

Now set all residual group coordinates to $u_i=1$ and take an open-orbit vector in the factor containing the bad summand. The support of that summand is empty there, by its definition and <a href="#eq:flag-test" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:flag-test">[eq:flag-test]</a>. Consequently every composite through it vanishes on this fiber. By <a href="#boundary:spherical-invariants" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="boundary:spherical-invariants">[boundary:spherical-invariants]</a>, the composite is independent of the raising vectors, so its value at the selected quotient classes is zero. It acts by zero on $S$, contradicting the preceding paragraph. Therefore every bad idempotent acts by zero. ◻

</details>

<a id="stmt-boundary-f-survives"></a>
<div id="boundary:f-survives" class="proposition">

**Proposition 48**. *For every finite initial segment of the selected places, $$q_\Pi^*f\ne0\quad\text{in }
 H^0\operatorname{RHom}(Q_\Pi,F_n^{\mathrm{ind}}).$$*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-boundary-f-survives"><summary>Proof of stmt-boundary-f-survives</summary>

<a id="boundary:cone-quotient"></a>
*Proof.* Apply $\operatorname{RHom}(-,F_n^{\mathrm{ind}})$ to the exterior product of the fiber diagrams <a href="#eq:boundary-test" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:boundary-test">[eq:boundary-test]</a>. Every term made from spherical and flag summands is a summand of $P$, so its mapping complex is concentrated in degree zero by Lemma <a href="#boundary:simple-quotient" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="boundary:simple-quotient">47</a>. The resulting finite total complex has its all-spherical term in degree zero and the terms with one bad factor in degree $-1$. Hence its zeroth cohomology is $$\label{boundary:cone-quotient}
 \frac{\operatorname{Hom}(B,F_n^{\mathrm{ind}})}{
 \displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^n\sum_{b\ \mathrm{bad}}
 \operatorname{im}\left[
 \operatorname{Hom}(B_1\boxtimes\cdots\boxtimes P_{i,b}\boxtimes\cdots\boxtimes B_n,
        F_n^{\mathrm{ind}})
 \longrightarrow\operatorname{Hom}(B,F_n^{\mathrm{ind}})\right]}.$$ The map from the all-spherical term to this quotient is pullback along $q_\Pi$; terms with two or more bad factors affect only negative degrees. The simple quotient $S$ of Lemma <a href="#boundary:simple-quotient" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="boundary:simple-quotient">47</a> annihilates every image in the denominator, since its corresponding bad idempotent acts by zero. It detects $f$ in the numerator. Thus the class of $f$ survives in <a href="#boundary:cone-quotient" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="boundary:cone-quotient">[boundary:cone-quotient]</a>, proving the proposition. ◻

</details>

<details id="proof-stmt-boundary-f-survives-2"><summary>Proof of stmt-boundary-f-survives</summary>

*Proof of Theorem <a href="#thm:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:main">2</a>.* Under the assumption that no enhancement exists, Lemma <a href="#lem:tensor-nilpotence" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:tensor-nilpotence">46</a> produced a finite set of selected places for which <a href="#boundary:f-vanishes" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="boundary:f-vanishes">[boundary:f-vanishes]</a> holds. The mapping comparison of Proposition <a href="#prop:ind-qc-comparison" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:ind-qc-comparison">42</a> gives the same vanishing in the Ind category, contradicting Proposition <a href="#boundary:f-survives" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="boundary:f-survives">48</a>. Therefore the assumption was false.

Proposition <a href="#prop:open-orbit-enhancement" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:open-orbit-enhancement">12</a> gives the precise resulting pair: its $\operatorname{SL}_2$-homomorphism has raising element in $\mathcal O_\nu$, its commuting Weil homomorphism has reductive closure and weight zero under every complex embedding, and their prescribed diagonal specialization equals the given $\sigma_\nu$ on the entire Weil group. That proposition also supplies continuity and a finite coefficient field. The argument used neither a restriction on the split semisimple type nor reducedness of the level divisor, so it applies to all the data of Theorem <a href="#thm:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:main">2</a>. ◻

</details>

  "figures": [
    {
      "caption": "The two uses of the global cyclic support. Full excursion tuple data identify the parameter when an open-orbit point exists. If every test sees boundary support, simultaneous coherent tests give incompatible vanishing and nonvanishing statements for the same cusp vector.",
      "figure_id": "fig:proof-architecture",
      "pdf_page": 7,
      "source_evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "fig:proof-architecture",
          "exact_quote": "The two uses of the global cyclic support.  Full excursion\n tuple data identify the parameter when an open-orbit point exists.\n If every test sees boundary support, simultaneous coherent tests\n give incompatible vanishing and nonvanishing s",
          "source_object": "fig:0001"
        }
      ],
      "source_kind": "other",
      "tikz_pgf_source": null,
      "witness_description": "A flow diagram arranged in two parallel vertical columns with a concluding sequence at the bottom:\n- Left column title: \"Global support\"\n- Right column title: \"Local tests and detection\"\n- Left column contains four vertically stacked rectangular boxes:\n  1. \"Occurring excursion character / Full Weil tuples; cusp vector $f \\neq 0$\"\n  2. \"One finite-type support $\\mathrm{Spec}\\, R$ / All tuple invariants match $\\sigma_{\\bar{v},\\mathrm{ad}}$ / Cyclic map $1 \\mapsto f$\"\n  3. \"Assume no global commuting pair / Every selected local value / then lies in the boundary\"\n  4. \"Boundary tests vanish on fibers / Many-place tensor nilpotence / $q_{\\bar{\\Pi}}^* f = 0$ for some finite product\"\n- Right column contains four vertically stacked rectangular boxes:\n  1. \"Enhanced Iwahori trace / Coherent Springer tests\"\n  2. \"One cohomological lower bound / valid simultaneously at / any finite set of selected places\"\n  3. \"Ind-coherent / quasi-coherent / mapping comparison\"\n  4. \"Simple cuspidal Hecke quotient / Bad flag idempotents act by zero / $q_{\\bar{\\Pi}}^* f \\neq 0$ for every finite product\"\n- Arrows between boxes:\n  - Vertical downward arrows connect box 1 to 2, 2 to 3, and 3 to 4 in the left column.\n  - Vertical downward arrows connect box 1 to 2, 2 to 3, and 3 to 4 in the right column.\n  - A long bypass arrow leaves the right side of box 1 of the right column, runs vertically down the right side, and points into the right side of box 4 of the right column.\n  - A diagonal downward arrow runs from the left side of box 3 of the right column to the right side of box 4 of the left column.\n- Bottom section:\n  - A centered rectangular box: \"Contradiction\", with incoming diagonal arrows from the bottom of box 4 of the left column and the bottom of box 4 of the right column.\n  - A vertical downward arrow leads from \"Contradiction\" to a final centered rectangular box: \"A global Arthur enhancement exists / Its diagonal specialization equals $\\sigma_{\\bar{v}}$ on the entire Weil group\"."
    },
    {
      "caption": "The hyperspecial unit is a retract of the Iwahori unit. After coherent duality, the Koszul functor $G_{0}$, and shear, this becomes the retract $B = 𝒪_{Y}$ of the flag object $P$. The dashed arrows record the objects corresponding under these operations; duality reverses the solid arrows. The comparison of representation-valued maps is [eq:hom-comparison], and the resulting internal algebra comparison is [eq:spherical-end].",
      "figure_id": "local:fig-retract",
      "pdf_page": 48,
      "source_evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "local:fig-retract",
          "exact_quote": "The hyperspecial unit is a retract of the Iwahori unit.\n After coherent duality, the Koszul functor $G_0$, and shear, this\n becomes the retract $B=\\cO_Y$ of the flag object $P$.\n The dashed arrows record the objects corresponding under thes",
          "source_object": "fig:0002"
        }
      ],
      "source_kind": "other",
      "tikz_pgf_source": null,
      "witness_description": "A commutative-style diagram with four rectangular nodes in two rows and two columns:\n- Top-left box: \"Hyperspecial unit / $[S(1)] \\simeq P_N^S$\"\n- Top-right box: \"Iwahori unit / $[1_I] \\simeq P_N$\"\n- Bottom-left box: \"Spherical summand / $B = \\mathcal{O}_Y$\"\n- Bottom-right box: \"Flag test object / $P = \\bigoplus_b P_b$\"\n- Arrows between top nodes:\n  - Upper horizontal hooked arrow from the top-left box to the top-right box, labeled \"$i_1$\" above.\n  - Lower horizontal arrow from the top-right box to the top-left box, labeled \"$c_K^{-1} l_1$\" below.\n- Arrows between bottom nodes:\n  - Upper horizontal arrow from the bottom-left box to the bottom-right box, labeled \"image of $c_K^{-1} l_1$\" above.\n  - Lower horizontal arrow from the bottom-right box to the bottom-left box, labeled \"image of $i_1$\" below.\n- Vertical connections:\n  - A vertical dashed arrow pointing downward from the top-left box to the bottom-left box.\n  - A vertical dashed arrow pointing downward from the top-right box to the bottom-right box.\n  - Centered text between the two vertical dashed arrows: \"On objects: / duality, $G_0$, and shear\"."
    }
  ],

      "direction": "directed",
      "explanation": "The global enhancement uses the finite-level nilpotent-orbit decomposition as its spectral input. The target supplies the orbit-indexed decomposition; the source states the local Arthur consequence required for global reconstruction.",
      "relation": "prerequisite",
      "source_anchor": "stmt-thm-parent",
      "source_pm_id": "PM-014.1",
      "source_quote": "For every occurring excursion character $\\nu$, there is a unique nilpotent $L$-orbit $\\mathcal O_\\nu\\subset\\operatorname{Lie}L$ such that, at every closed point $x\\in U$, the semisimple class of $\\sigma_\\nu(\\operatorname{Frob}_x)$ has the form $$\\label{intro:local-arthur}\n \\left[\n  \\phi_{\\mathcal O_\\nu}\n      \\begin{pmatrix}a^{d_x}&0\\\\0&a^{-d_x}\\end{pmatrix}c_x\n \\right].$$ Here $\\phi_{\\mathcal O_\\nu}:\\operatorname{SL}_2\\to L$ is a Jacobson–Morozov homomorphism for $\\mathcal O_\\nu$ and $c_x$ is semisimple, centralizes its image, and is algebraic and conjugate into a compact subgroup at every complex embedding. The same orbit occurs at every $x$.",
